<!-- dfn-1: body_md -->
\label{D_Cuntz_Subeq}
Let $A$ be a C*-algebra.
 
\begin{enumerate}
 
\item
For $a,b \in (A \otimes K)_+$, we say that $a$ is
\emph{Cuntz subequivalent} to $b$ over $A$, written
$a \precsim_A b$, if there is a sequence $(v_{n})_{n=1}^{\infty}$
in $A \otimes K$ such that $\lim_{n \to \infty} v_n b v_n^* = a$.
 
\item
We say that $a$ and $b$ are \emph{Cuntz equivalent} in $A$,
written $a \sim_{A} b$, if $a \precsim_{A} b$ and $b \precsim_{A} a$.
This relation is an equivalence relation, and we write $\langle a \rangle_A$
for the equivalence class of $a$ in $A$.
When there is no
possibility of confusion, we sometimes just write
$a \precsim b$, $a \sim b$, and $\langle a \rangle$.
 
\item
The \emph{Cuntz semigroup} of $A$ is
 
\[
{\operatorname{Cu}} (A) = (A \otimes K)_{+} / \sim_{A},
\]
 
together with the semigroup operation $\langle a \rangle_A + \langle b \rangle_A =
\langle a \oplus b \rangle_A$ and the partial order given by
$\langle a \rangle_A \leq \langle b \rangle_A$ if and only if $a \precsim_{A} b$.
 
\item
We define a subsemigroup ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ of ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ by
 
\[
{\operatorname{W}} (A) = M_{\infty}(A)_{+} / \sim_{A},
\]
 
which is the ``classical'' Cuntz semigroup of $A$.
We also denote the class of $a$ in ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ by~$\langle a \rangle_A$,
except in Remark~\ref{1903_rem}
and Section~\ref{SubSec_WA}, where we use~$[a]_A$.
 
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end dfn-1 -->
<!-- rmk-1: body_md -->
\label{1903_rem}
The operations and order in ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ are the same as in ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$.
By Remark~1.2 of \cite{Phl40},
if $a,b \in M_{\infty}(A)_+$, then $\langle a \rangle_A \leq \langle b \rangle_A$ in ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$
if and only if $[a]_A \leq [b]_A$ in ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$.
The notational distinction between $\langle a \rangle_A$ and $[a]_A$ will only be
used in Section~\ref{SubSec_WA}.
<!-- end rmk-1 -->
<!-- dfn-2: body_md -->
\label{LeqS}
For an ordered semigroup $S$ and $\xi,\eta \in S$, write
$\xi <_{\operatorname{s}} \eta$ if $(k + 1)\xi \leq k \eta$ for some $k \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$.
<!-- end dfn-2 -->
<!-- dfn-3: body_md -->
\label{nCompDefn}
An ordered semigroup $S$ has \emph{$n$-comparison} if
given $\xi, \eta_{0}, \ldots, \eta_{n} \in S$,
the relation $\xi <_{\operatorname{s}} \eta_{j}$
for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, n$ implies $\xi \leq \sum_{j=0}^{n} \eta_{j}$.
<!-- end dfn-3 -->
<!-- dfn-4: body_md -->
\label{nCompA}
Let $A$ be a C*-algebra.
We say $A$ has \emph{$n$-comparison}
if ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ has $n$-comparison in the sense of Definition
\ref{nCompDefn}.
If $A$ has 0-comparison, we say that $A$ has
{\emph{strict comparison}}.
<!-- end dfn-4 -->
<!-- dfn-5: body_md -->
\label{D_Cu_WayBelow}
Let $S$ be an ordered semigroup, and let $\xi, \xi' \in S$.
We write $\xi' \ll \xi$ if for every increasing sequence
$(\xi_n)$ in $S$ satisfying $\xi \leq \sup_n \xi_n$, there
is $N \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$ such that $\xi' \leq \xi_N$.
We call $\ll$
the \emph{way below} relation (or \emph{compact containment}
relation) and say \emph{$\xi'$ is way below $\xi$}
(or \emph{$\xi'$ is compactly contained in $\xi$}).
<!-- end dfn-5 -->
<!-- dfn-6: body_md -->
\label{D_Cu_Semigp}
An ordered semigroup $S$ is said to be a \emph{${\operatorname{Cu}}$-semigroup}
if the following properties hold:
 
\begin{enumerate}
 
\item
If $(\xi_n)$ is an increasing sequence in $S$,
then $\sup_n \xi_n$ exists.
 
\item
For any $\xi \in S$ there exists a sequence $(\xi_n)$
such that $\xi_n \ll \xi_{n+1}$ for all $n$ and
$\xi = \sup_n \xi_n$.
 
\item
If $\xi_1 \ll \xi_2$ and $\eta_1 \ll \eta_2$ in $S$,
then $\xi_1 + \eta_1 \ll \xi_2 + \eta_2$.
 
\item
If $(\xi_n)$ and $(\eta_n)$ are increasing sequences
in $S$, then $\sup_n (\xi_n + \eta_n) = \sup_n \xi_n + \sup_n \eta_n$.
 
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end dfn-6 -->
<!-- dfn-7: body_md -->
\label{def_almost_divisibility}
Let $S$ be a ${\operatorname{Cu}}$-semigroup, and let $m \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$.
We say that $S$ is \emph{$m$-almost divisible} if
for any $\xi, \xi' \in S$ such that $\xi' \ll \xi$
and any $N \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$, there exists $\eta \in S$ such that
 
\[
N \eta \leq \xi \quad{\mbox{and}}\quad \xi' \leq
(N + 1) (m + 1) \eta.
\]
 
We say that $S$ is \emph{almost divisible} if $S$ is
$0$-almost divisible.
<!-- end dfn-7 -->
<!-- dfn-8: body_md -->
\label{D_AlmostDiv_Alg}
Let $A$ be a C*-algebra, and let $m \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$.
We say that
$A$ is \emph{$m$-almost divisible} if ${\operatorname{Cu}}(A)$ is $m$-almost
divisible in the sense of
Definition~\ref{def_almost_divisibility}.
We say that $A$ is
\emph{almost divisible} if $A$ is 0-almost divisible.
<!-- end dfn-8 -->
<!-- dfn-9: body_md -->
\label{D_PureAlg}
Let $A$ be a C*-algebra and let $m,n \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$.
We say that
$A$ is \emph{$(n,m)$-pure} if has $n$-comparison in the
sense of Definition~\ref{nCompA} and is $m$-almost divisible
in the sense of Definition~\ref{def_almost_divisibility}.
We say that $A$ is \emph{pure} if it is $(0,0)$-pure.
<!-- end dfn-9 -->
<!-- thm-1: body_md -->
\label{T_PureEquiv}
Given a C*-algebra $A$, the following are equivalent:
 
\begin{enumerate}
 
\item
$A$ is pure; that is, $A$ has strict comparison and is almost divisible.
 
\item
$A$ is $(n,m)$-pure for some $m,n \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$.
 
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end thm-1 -->
<!-- lem-1: body_md -->
\label{L_3618_Sp01}
Let $A$ be a simple C*-algebra{} which is not of Type~I.
Then there exists $a \in A_{+}$ such that
${\operatorname{sp}} (a) = [0, 1]$.
<!-- end lem-1 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
The discussion before~(1) on page~61 of~\cite{AkmnShlz}
shows that $A$ is not scattered in the sense of~\cite{AkmnShlz}.
The conclusion therefore follows
from the argument in~(4) on page~61 of \cite{AkmnShlz}.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lem-2: body_md -->
\label{L_3618_Lg36}
Let $A$ be a simple stably finite unital C*-algebra which
is not of Type I.
Let $p \in A \otimes K$ be a nonzero
projection, let $N \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$, and let $\xi \in {\operatorname{Cu}} (A) \setminus \left\{ 0 \right\}$.
Then there exist $\mu, \kappa \in {\operatorname{W}} (A)$ which are not the classes of projection{s}
and such that $\mu \leq \langle p \rangle \leq \mu + \kappa$ and $N \kappa \leq \xi$.
<!-- end lem-2 -->
<!-- lem-3: body_md -->
\label{L_5Z26_InterpPj}
Let $A$ be a simple stably finite unital C*-algebra{} which is not of
Type~I.
Let $p \in A \otimes K$ be a nonzero projection,
let $N \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$,
and let $\xi \in {\operatorname{Cu}} (A) \setminus \{ 0 \}$.
Then there exist $x, y \in (p (A \otimes K) p)_{+}$ and
$\varepsilon \in (0, 1)$ such that:
 
\begin{enumerate}
 
\item\label{I_L_5Z26_InnPj_Spec}
${\operatorname{sp}} (x) = {\operatorname{sp}} (y) = [0, 1]$.
 
\item\label{I_L_5Z26_InnPj_xlp}
$x \precsim_A p$.
 
\item\label{I_L_5Z26_InnPj_plxy}
$p \precsim_A (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \oplus (y - \varepsilon)_{+}$.
 
\item\label{I_L_5Z26_InnPj_nylxi}
$N \langle y \rangle_A \leq \xi$.
 
\item\label{I_L_5Z26_InnPj_NyLeBe}
$N \langle y \rangle_A \leq \langle (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_A$.
 
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-3 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Choose $a \in (A \otimes K)_{+} \setminus \{ 0 \}$
such that $\langle a \rangle_A = \xi$.
Use Lemma 2.4 of~\cite{Phl40} to choose
orthogonal nonzero positive elements
$b_0, b_1, b_2, \ldots, b_N \in p (A \otimes K) p$
such that $b_0 \sim_A b_1 \sim_A b_2 \sim_A \cdots \sim_A b_N$
and orthogonal nonzero positive elements
$c_1, c_2, c_3 \ldots, c_N \in {\overline{a (A \otimes K) a}}$
such that $c_1 \sim_A c_2 \sim_A \cdots \sim_A c_N$.
By Lemma 2.6 of~\cite{Phl40}, there is
$d \in
  \bigl( {\overline{b_0 (A \otimes K) b_0}} \bigr)_{+}
  \setminus \left\{ 0 \right\}$
such that $d \precsim_A c_1$.
Use Lemma~\ref{L_3618_Sp01} to choose
$y \in \bigl( {\overline{d (A \otimes K) d}} \bigr)_{+}$
such that ${\operatorname{sp}} (y) = [0, 1]$.
Define $x = p - y$ and $\varepsilon = \frac{1}{3}$.
We show that these choices satisfy the conclusion of the lemma.

Part~(\ref{I_L_5Z26_InnPj_Spec}) is clear.
For~(\ref{I_L_5Z26_InnPj_xlp}), we in fact have $x \leq p$.
For~(\ref{I_L_5Z26_InnPj_nylxi}), we have
\[
y \precsim_A d \precsim_A c_1
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
N \langle c_1 \rangle_A = \langle c_1 \rangle_A + \langle c_2 \rangle_A + \cdots +
\langle c_N \rangle_A \leq \langle a \rangle_A = \xi.
\]
We prove~(\ref{I_L_5Z26_InnPj_plxy}).
Set $r = (x - \varepsilon)_{+} + (y - \varepsilon)_{+} \in p (A \otimes K) p$.
Then
\[
\| r - p \|
\leq \bigl\| (x - \varepsilon)_{+} - x \bigr\|
  + \bigl\| (y - \varepsilon)_{+} - y \bigr\|
\leq \frac{1}{3} + \frac{1}{3}
= \frac{2}{3}.
\]
Therefore $r$ is invertible in $p (A \otimes K) p$.
So
\[
p \sim_A r = (x - \varepsilon)_{+} + (y - \varepsilon)_{+}
\precsim_A (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \oplus (y - \varepsilon)_{+},
\]
as desired.

Finally, we prove~(\ref{I_L_5Z26_InnPj_NyLeBe}).
Define a continuous function{} $f \colon [0, \infty) \to [0, 1]$ by
\[
f ( \lambda) = \begin{cases}
0 & \hspace*{1em} 0 \leq \lambda \leq \frac{1}{3} \\
3 \lambda - 1 & \hspace*{1em} \frac{1}{3} < \lambda \leq \frac{2}{3}\\
1 & \hspace*{1em} \frac{2}{3} < \lambda.
\end{cases}
\]
Then $(x - \varepsilon)_{+} \sim f (x)$.
Since
\[
p - x = y \in {\overline{d (A \otimes K) d}}
\subseteq {\overline{b_0 (A \otimes K) b_0}},
\]
we have $x b_j = b_j$ for $j = 1, 2, \ldots, N$.
Therefore also $f (x) b_j = b_j$ for $j = 1, 2, \ldots, N$.
Now
\[
N \langle b_0 \rangle_A = \langle b_1 \rangle_A + \langle b_2 \rangle_A + \cdots +
\langle b_N \rangle_A \leq \langle f (x) \rangle_A = \langle (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_A.
\]
Since
$y \in {\overline{b_0 (A \otimes K) b_0}}$,
we have $y \precsim_A b_0$,
and (\ref{I_L_5Z26_InnPj_NyLeBe}) follows.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- prp-1: body_md -->
\label{CP_6325_WA_Cmp}
Let $A$ be a C*-algebra{} and let $n \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$.
Then ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ has $n$-comparison
if and only if ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ has $n$-comparison.
<!-- end prp-1 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
Let us first assume that ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ has $n$-comparison.
Let $a, a_0, a_1, \hdots, a_n \in M_{\infty}(A)_+$ satisfy
$[a]_A <_{\operatorname{s}} [a_j]$ in ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ for $j = 0, 1, \hdots, n$.
Thus, there are $k_0, k_1, \hdots, k_n \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}$
such that in ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ we have
 
\begin{equation*}
(k_j + 1) [a]_A \leq k_j [a_j]_A.
\end{equation*}
 
For $j = 0, 1, \hdots, n$, we have
$(k_j + 1) \langle a \rangle_A \leq k_j \langle a_j \rangle_A$ in ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$.
By $n$-comparison for ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$,
 
\begin{equation*}
\langle a \rangle_A \leq \sum_{j = 0}^n \langle a_j \rangle_A.
\end{equation*}
 
By Remark~\ref{1903_rem}, it follows that in ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ we have
\[
[a]_A \leq \sum_{j = 0}^n [a_j]_A,
\]
showing $n$-comparison for ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$.

Let us now assume that ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ has $n$-comparison.
Let $a, a_0, a_1, \hdots, a_n \in (A \otimes K)_+$,
and suppose that there are $k_0, k_1, \hdots, k_n \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}$ such that
 
\begin{equation*}
(k_j + 1) \langle a \rangle_A \leq k_j \langle a_j \rangle_A
\end{equation*}
 
for $j = 0, 1, \hdots, n$.
Let $\varepsilon > 0$.
Choose $\delta_j > 0$ such that in ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ we have
\[
(k_j + 1) \langle (a - \varepsilon)_+ \rangle_A  \leq k_j \langle (a_j- \delta_j)_+ \rangle_A.
\]
By Lemma~1.9 of~\cite{Phl40}, there exist
$c, c_0, c_1, \hdots, c_n \in M_{\infty}(A)_+$ such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{2003_comp_eqn_Prv}
\langle (a - \varepsilon)_+ \rangle_A = \langle c \rangle_A
\end{equation}
 
and for $j = 0, 1, \hdots, n$ we have
 
\begin{equation}\label{2003_comp_eqn}
\langle (a_j- \delta_j) \rangle_A = \langle c_j \rangle_A.
\end{equation}
 
Thus, in ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ we have
\[
(k_j + 1) \langle c \rangle_A \leq k_j \langle c_j \rangle_A
\]
and hence, by Remark~\ref{1903_rem}, in ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ we have
\[
(k_j + 1) [c]_A \leq k_j [c_j]_A.
\]
By $n$-comparison for ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$, we see that in ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ we have
 
\begin{equation}\label{2003_comp_eqn_w}
[c]_A \leq \sum_{j = 0}^n [c_j]_A.
\end{equation}
 
Using (\ref{2003_comp_eqn_Prv}) at the first step,
(\ref{2003_comp_eqn_w}) and Remark~\ref{1903_rem} at the second step,
and (\ref{2003_comp_eqn}) at the third step,
we get the following inequalities in ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$:
\[
\langle (a - \varepsilon)_+ \rangle_A = \langle c \rangle_A \leq \sum_{j = 0}^n \langle c_j \rangle_A
 = \sum_{j = 0}^n \langle (a_j- \delta_j) \rangle_A \leq \sum_{j = 0}^n \langle a_j \rangle_A.
\]
As $\varepsilon > 0$ is arbitrary, we have
$\langle a \rangle_A \leq \sum_{j = 0}^n \langle a_j \rangle_A$,
proving $n$-comparison for ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- lem-4: body_md -->
\label{L_5Z28_CptCont}
Let $A$ be a C*-algebra{} and let $a, b \in (A \otimes K)_{+}$.
Then $\langle a \rangle_A \ll \langle b \rangle_A$ if and only if{} there is an $\varepsilon > 0$ such
that $a \precsim_A (b - \varepsilon)_{+}$.
<!-- end lem-4 -->
<!-- prp-2: body_md -->
\label{P_6324_WDiv}
Let $A$ be a C*-algebra{} and let $m \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$.
Then ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ is $m$-almost divisible if and only if
for every $a \in M_{\infty} (A)_+$, every $k \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}$, and every $\varepsilon > 0$,
there exists $b \in M_{\infty} (A)_+$ such that in ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ we have
\[
k [b]_A \leq [a]_A
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
[(a - \varepsilon)_+]_A \leq (k + 1) (m + 1) [b]_A.
\]
<!-- end prp-2 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
Let ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ be $m$-almost divisible.
Let $a \in M_{\infty}(A)_+$ and let $k \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}$.
Let $\varepsilon > 0$ be arbitrary.
Since
 
$\left\langle \left( a - \frac{2 \varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ \right\rangle_A
 \ll \left\langle \left(a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ \right\rangle_A$,
 
and ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ is $m$-almost divisible,
there exists $b \in (A \otimes K)_+$ such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{1903_div_wtc}
k \langle b \rangle_A \leq \left\langle \left(a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ \right\rangle_A
\quad {\mbox{and}} \quad
\left\langle \left(a - \frac{2 \varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ \right\rangle_A \leq (k + 1) (m + 1) \langle b \rangle_A.
\end{equation}
 
Choose $\delta > 0$ such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{1903_cutdown_wtc}
\left\langle \left(a - \varepsilon\right)_+ \right\rangle_A \leq (k + 1) (m + 1) \langle (b- \delta)_+ \rangle_A.
\end{equation}
 
By Lemma~1.9 of~\cite{Phl40},
there exists $c\in M_{\infty}(A)_+$ such that
\[
\langle (b- \delta)_+ \rangle_A = \langle c \rangle_A.
\]
Hence, by (\ref{1903_div_wtc}) and (\ref{1903_cutdown_wtc}), we see that
 
\begin{equation*}
k \langle c \rangle_A \leq \langle a \rangle_A
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
\langle (a - \varepsilon)_+ \rangle_A \leq (k + 1) (m + 1) \langle c \rangle_A.
\end{equation*}
 
Finally, by Remark~\ref{1903_rem}, it follows that in ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ we have
\[
k [c]_A \leq [a]_A
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
[(a - \varepsilon)_+] \leq (k + 1) (m + 1) [c]_A.
\]
This is the desired relation.

Conversely, assume that for all $\varepsilon > 0$,
$k \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}$, and $a \in M_{\infty}(A)_+$,
there exists $b \in M_{\infty}(A)_+$ such that in ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ we have
\[
k[b]_A \leq [a]_A
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
[(a - \varepsilon)_+] \leq (k + 1) (m + 1) [b]_A.
\]
Let $k \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}$ and let $\xi, \eta \in {\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ satisfy $\xi \ll \eta$.
Choose $a \in (A \otimes K)_+$ such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6327_eta}
\langle a \rangle_A = \eta.
\end{equation}
 
By Lemma~\ref{L_5Z28_CptCont}, there is $\varepsilon > 0$ such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{1903_almost_div_ctw}
\xi \leq \langle (a - \varepsilon)_+ \rangle_A.
\end{equation}
 
By Lemma~1.9 of~\cite{Phl40},
there exists $ c\in M_{\infty}(A)_+$ such that
\begin{equation*}
\langle c \rangle_A = \left\langle \left(a - \frac{\varepsilon}{2} \right)_+ \right\rangle_A.
\end{equation*}
Choose $\delta > 0$ such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6327_NN}
\left(a - \varepsilon\right)_+ \precsim_A (c- \delta)_+.
\end{equation}
 
By hypothesis, there exists $b \in M_{\infty}(A)_+$ such that
in ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ we have
\[
k[b]_A \leq [c]_A
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
\left[\left(c- \delta\right)_+\right]_A \leq (k + 1) (m + 1) [b]_A.
\]
In ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ we now get,
using Remark~\ref{1903_rem} and~(\ref{Eq_6327_eta}),
 
\begin{equation*}
k \langle b \rangle_A \leq \langle c \rangle_A
 = \left\langle \left(a - \frac{\varepsilon}{2} \right)_+ \right\rangle_A \leq \eta
\end{equation*}
 
and using (\ref{1903_almost_div_ctw}), (\ref{Eq_6327_NN}),
and Remark~\ref{1903_rem},
 
\begin{equation*}
\xi \leq \langle (a - \varepsilon)_+ \rangle_A
 \leq  \left\langle \left(c- \delta\right)_+ \right\rangle_A \leq (k + 1) (m + 1) \langle c \rangle_A.
\end{equation*}
 
This proves $m$-almost divisibility for ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- ntn-1: body_md -->
\label{N_6315_Cup}
Let $A$ be a stably finite simple unital C*-algebra.
Following Definition~3.1 of~\cite{Phl40},
we let ${\operatorname{Cu}}_{+} (A)$ denote the set of $\eta \in {\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ such that
$\eta$ is not the class of a projection;
that is, $\eta$ is purely positive in the sense of~\cite{APT}.
See also \cite{EllRoSa}.
<!-- end ntn-1 -->
<!-- thm-2: body_md -->
\label{T_6318_Isom_CuPl}
Let $A$ be an infinite dimensional stably finite simple
unital C*-algebra, and let $B \subseteq A$ be a subalgebra
which is large in the sense of Definition~4.1 of~\cite{Phl40}.
Let $\iota \colon B \to A$ be the inclusion map.
Then $\iota_{*}$ defines an order and semigroup isomorphism
from ${\operatorname{Cu}}_{+}(B) \cup \left\{ 0 \right\}$ to ${\operatorname{Cu}}_{+}(A) \cup \left\{ 0 \right\}$.
<!-- end thm-2 -->
<!-- cor-1: body_md -->
\label{C_6318_LgCmp}
Let the assumptions be as in Theorem~\ref{T_6318_Isom_CuPl}.
Then for any $x \in (A \otimes K)_{+}$ for which
$\langle x \rangle_A$ is not the class of a projection in ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$,
there is a $y \in (B \otimes K)_{+}$ such that $x \sim_{A} y$
and $\langle y \rangle_B$ is not the class of projection in ${\operatorname{Cu}} (B)$.
<!-- end cor-1 -->
<!-- lem-5: body_md -->
\label{L_3618_LgSp01}
Let $A$ be an infinite dimensional stably finite simple
unital C*-algebra, let $B \subseteq A$ be a subalgebra
which is large in the sense of Definition~4.1 of~\cite{Phl40}
(possibly $B = A$),
and let $C \subseteq B$ be a nonzero hereditary subalgebra.
Then there exists $a \in C_{+}$ such that
${\operatorname{sp}} (a) = [0, 1]$.
<!-- end lem-5 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
The algebra $B$ is simple by Proposition~5.2 of~\cite{Phl40},
infinite dimensional by Proposition~5.5 of~\cite{Phl40},
and unital.
Therefore $B$ is not of Type~I, so $C$ is also is not of Type~I.
Thus Lemma~\ref{L_3618_Sp01} applies.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- thm-3: body_md -->
\label{T_6314_CompThm}
Let $A$ be a simple separable infinite dimensional stably
finite unital C*-algebra, let $B \subseteq A$ be a subalgebra
which is large in the sense of Definition~4.1 of~\cite{Phl40},
and let $m \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$.
Then $B$ has $m$-comparison if and only if $A$ has $m$-comparison.
<!-- end thm-3 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
We begin by showing that $m$-comparison for ${\operatorname{Cu}} (B)$ implies
$m$-comparison for ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$.
Let $x, a_{0}, a_{1}, \ldots, a_{m} \in (A \otimes K)_{+}$
satisfy $\langle x \rangle_A <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle a_{j} \rangle_A$ for
$j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$.
We must show that
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6315_Cmp_to_pr}
\langle x \rangle_A \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle a_j \rangle_A.
\end{equation}
 
If $\langle x \rangle_A = 0$, this is immediate, so we assume
that $\langle x \rangle_A \neq 0$.
By the definition of $<_{\operatorname{s}}$ (Definition~\ref{LeqS}),
for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$ there is $k_j \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$
such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6318_M_SubS}
(k_j + 1) \langle x \rangle_A \leq k_j \langle a_j \rangle_A.
\end{equation}
 

First assume $\langle x \rangle_A$ is not the class of a projection.
Use Corollary~\ref{C_6318_LgCmp}
to choose $y \in (B \otimes K)_{+}$ such that $\langle y \rangle_A = \langle x \rangle_A$
and $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (y)$.
Let $\varepsilon > 0$ be arbitrary. For $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$,
we will find $b_j \in (B \otimes K)_{+}$ such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6318_L}
\langle (y - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_B <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle b_j \rangle_B
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
\langle b_j \rangle_A \leq \langle a_j \rangle_A.
\end{equation}
 
Since $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (y)$,
there is $\rho \in (0, \varepsilon)$ such that
${\operatorname{sp}} (y) \cap (\rho, \varepsilon) \neq \varnothing$.
Choose $\lambda \in {\operatorname{sp}} (y) \cap (\rho, \varepsilon)$.
Choose a continuous function{} $f \colon [0, \infty) \to [0, 1]$ such that
${\operatorname{supp}} (f) \subseteq (\rho, \varepsilon)$ and $f (\lambda) \neq 0$.

Define
\[
I = \bigl\{ j \in \{0, 1, \ldots, m\} \colon
 {\mbox{$\langle a_j \rangle_A$ is the class of a nonzero projection}} \bigr\}.
\]

For $j \not\in I$, Corollary~\ref{C_6318_LgCmp} provides
$b_j \in (B \otimes K)_{+}$ such that
$\langle b_j \rangle_A = \langle a_j \rangle_A$ in ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$.
Use Lemma~\ref{L_3618_LgSp01} to choose
$c \in \bigl( {\overline{f (y) (B \otimes K) f (y)}} \bigr)_{+}$
such that ${\operatorname{sp}} (c) = [0, 1]$.
Set $z_j = (y - \varepsilon)_{+} + c$.
Then $(y - \varepsilon)_{+} \precsim_B z_j \precsim_B y$.
We have
\[
(k_j + 1) \langle z_j \rangle_A
 \leq (k_j + 1) \langle y \rangle_A
 = (k_j + 1) \langle x \rangle_A
 \leq k_j \langle a_j \rangle_A
 = k_j \langle b_j \rangle_A.
\]
Since $0$ is a limit point of both ${\operatorname{sp}} (z_j)$ and ${\operatorname{sp}} (b_j)$,
Theorem~\ref{T_6318_Isom_CuPl} gives the second step in
the calculation
\[
(k_j + 1) \langle (y - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_B
 \leq (k_j + 1) \langle z_j \rangle_B
 \leq k_j \langle b_j \rangle_B.
\]
It follows that $\langle (y - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_B <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle b_j \rangle_B$,
and we already have $\langle b_j \rangle_A = \langle a_j \rangle_A$.
So~(\ref{Eq_6318_L}) holds.

Now suppose $j \in I$.
We may as well assume that $a_j$ is a projection.
Use Lemma~\ref{L_5Z26_InterpPj} to choose
$r_j, s_j \in (a_j (A \otimes K) a_j)_{+}$ and $\delta \in (0, 1)$
such that
\[
{\operatorname{sp}} (r_j) = {\operatorname{sp}} (s_j) = [0, 1],
\qquad
r_j \precsim_A a_j,
\]
\[
a_j \precsim_A (r_j - \delta)_{+} \oplus (s_j - \delta)_{+},
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
2 k_j \langle s_j \rangle_A \leq \langle (r_j - \delta)_{+} \rangle_A.
\]
Corollary~\ref{C_6318_LgCmp} yields $b_j \in (B \otimes K)_{+}$
such that $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (b_j)$ and $\langle b_j \rangle_A = \langle r_j \rangle_A$.
Use Lemma~2.6 of~\cite{Phl40} to choose
$d_j \in \bigl( {\overline{f (y) (A \otimes K) f (y)}} \bigr)_{+}
    \setminus \{ 0 \}$
such that $d_j \precsim_A s_j$.
Use Lemma~\ref{L_3618_LgSp01} to choose
$g_j \in \bigl( {\overline{d_j (A \otimes K) d_j}} \bigr)_{+}$
such that ${\operatorname{sp}} (g_j) = [0, 1]$.
Corollary~\ref{C_6318_LgCmp} yields $c_j \in (K \otimes B)_{+}$
such that $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (c_j)$ and $\langle c_j \rangle_A = \langle g_j \rangle_A$.
Set $z_j = (y - \varepsilon)_{+} \oplus c_j$.
We have $c_j \precsim_A f (y)$, so
\[
\begin{split}
(2 k_j + 2) \langle z_j \rangle_A
& \leq (2 k_j + 2) \langle y \rangle_A
  = (2 k_j + 2) \langle x \rangle_A
  \leq 2 k_j \langle a_j \rangle_A
\\
& \leq 2 k_j \langle (r_j - \delta)_{+} \rangle_A + 2 k_j \langle (s_j - \delta)_{+} \rangle_A
\\
& \leq (2 k_j + 1) \langle r_j \rangle_A
  = (2 k_j + 1) \langle b_j \rangle_A.
\end{split}
\]
Since $0$ is a limit point of both ${\operatorname{sp}} (z_j)$ and ${\operatorname{sp}} (b_j)$,
Theorem~\ref{T_6318_Isom_CuPl} gives the second step in
the calculation
\[
(2 k_j + 2) \langle (y - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_B
 \leq (2 k_j + 2) \langle z_j \rangle_B
 \leq (2 k_j + 1) \langle b_j \rangle_B.
\]
It follows that $\langle (y - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_B <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle b_j \rangle_B$.
We already have $\langle b_j \rangle_A = \langle r_j \rangle_A \leq \langle a_j \rangle_A$.
So~(\ref{Eq_6318_L}) holds in this case too.

Since (\ref{Eq_6318_L}) holds for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$
and $B$ has $m$-comparison, it follows that
\[
\langle (y - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_B \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle b_j \rangle_B.
\]
Using the second part of~(\ref{Eq_6318_L}), we get
\[
\langle (y - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_A
 \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle b_j \rangle_A
 \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle a_j \rangle_A.
\]
Since $\varepsilon > 0$ is arbitrary, we get
\[
\langle y \rangle_A \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle a_j \rangle_A.
\]
Combining this with $\langle x \rangle_A = \langle y \rangle_A$ gives~(\ref{Eq_6315_Cmp_to_pr})
and completes the proof of this direction in the case that $\langle x \rangle_A$
is not the class of a projection.

Now assume that $\langle x \rangle_A$ is the class of a nonzero
projection in $A \otimes K$.
Since $x \neq 0$, we have $a_j \neq 0$ for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$.
Use Lemma 2.6 of \cite{Phl40} to choose
$a \in (A \otimes K)_{+} \setminus \{0\}$ such that
$\langle a \rangle_A \leq \langle a_j \rangle_A$ for $j = 1, 2, \hdots, m$.
Set $k = \max ( k_0, k_1, \ldots, k_m )$.
By Lemma~\ref{L_3618_Lg36}, there exist $\mu, \eta \in {\operatorname{Cu}}_{+} (A)$
such that
\[
\mu \leq \langle x \rangle_A \leq \mu + \eta
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
(2 k + 2) \eta \leq \langle a \rangle_A.
\]
For $j = 0, 1, \hdots, m$, we then have,
also using (\ref{Eq_6318_M_SubS}) at the second step,
\[
\begin{split}
(2 k_j + 2) (\mu + \eta)
& \leq (2 k_j + 2) \langle x \rangle_A + (2 k_j + 2) \eta
\\
& \leq 2 k_j \langle a_j \rangle_A + \langle a \rangle_A
  \leq 2 k_j \langle a_j \rangle_A + \langle a_j \rangle_A
  = (2 k_j + 1) \langle a_j \rangle_A.
\end{split}
\]
Hence $\mu + \eta <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle a_j \rangle_A$.
Since $\mu + \eta \in {\operatorname{Cu}}_{+} (A)$, the previous case yields
the second step in the calculation
\[
\langle x \rangle_A \leq \mu + \eta \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle a_j \rangle_A,
\]
which is~(\ref{Eq_6315_Cmp_to_pr}).
This completes the proof that if ${\operatorname{Cu}} (B)$ has
$m$-comparison, then so does ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$.

The reverse implication uses the same pieces, but arranged differently.
Assume that ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ has $m$-comparison;
we show that ${\operatorname{Cu}} (B)$ has $m$-comparison as well.
To this end, let
$y, b_0, b_1, \ldots, b_m \in (B \otimes K)_{+}$ satisfy
$\langle y \rangle_B <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle b_j \rangle_B$ for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$.
Without loss of generality{} $y \neq 0$.
By the definition of $<_{\operatorname{s}}$ (Definition~\ref{LeqS}),
for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$ there is $k_j \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$
such that $(k_j + 1) \langle y \rangle_B \leq k_j \langle b_j \rangle_B$.
In particular, $b_j \neq 0$ for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$.

First assume $\langle y \rangle_B$ is not the class of a projection.
Let $\varepsilon > 0$ be arbitrary. We will find $z \in (B \otimes K)_{+}$
and for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$, we will find $a_j \in (B \otimes K)_{+}$
such that $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (z)$,
$(y - \varepsilon)_{+} \precsim_B z$,
and, for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$, $\langle z \rangle_A <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle a_j \rangle_A$,
$0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (a_j)$, and $\langle a_j \rangle_B \leq \langle b_j \rangle_B$.

Since $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (y)$,
there is $\rho \in (0, \varepsilon)$ such that
${\operatorname{sp}} (y) \cap (\rho, \varepsilon) \neq \varnothing$.
Choose $\lambda \in {\operatorname{sp}} (y) \cap (\rho, \varepsilon)$.
Choose a continuous function{} $f \colon [0, \infty) \to [0, 1]$ such that
${\operatorname{supp}} (f) \subseteq (\rho, \varepsilon)$ and $f (\lambda) \neq 0$.
Use Lemma~\ref{L_3618_LgSp01} to choose
$c \in \bigl( {\overline{f (y) (B \otimes K) f (y)}} \bigr)_{+}$
such that ${\operatorname{sp}} (c) = [0, 1]$.
Set $z = (y - \varepsilon)_{+} + c$.
Then $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (z)$ and
$(y - \varepsilon)_{+} \precsim_B z \precsim_B y$.

Define
\[
I = \bigl\{ j \in \{0, 1, \ldots, m\} \colon
 {\mbox{$\langle b_j \rangle_B$ is the class of a nonzero projection}} \bigr\}.
\]

For $j \not\in I$, set $a_j = b_j$.
Then $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (a_j)$.
Also,
\[
\langle z \rangle_B \leq \langle y \rangle_B
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
(k_j + 1) \langle y \rangle_B \leq k_j \langle a_j \rangle_B.
\]
The second says that $\langle z \rangle_B <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle a_j \rangle_B$.

Now suppose $j \in I$.
We may as well assume that $b_j$ is a projection.
Use Lemma~\ref{L_5Z26_InterpPj} to choose
$a_j, s_j \in (b_j (B \otimes K) b_j)_{+}$ and $\delta \in (0, 1)$
such that
\[
{\operatorname{sp}} (a_j) = {\operatorname{sp}} (s_j) = [0, 1],
\qquad
a_j \precsim_B b_j,
\]
\[
b_j \precsim_B (a_j - \delta)_{+} \oplus (s_j - \delta)_{+},
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
2 k_j \langle s_j \rangle_B \leq \langle (a_j - \delta)_{+} \rangle_B.
\]
Then
\[
\begin{split}
(2 k_j + 2) \langle z \rangle_B
& \leq (2 k_j + 2) \langle y \rangle_B
\\
& \leq 2 k_j \langle b_j \rangle_B
  \leq 2 k_j \langle a_j \rangle_B + 2 k_j \langle s_j \rangle_B
  \leq (2 k_j + 1) \langle a_j \rangle_B.
\end{split}
\]
Thus, here too $\langle z \rangle_B <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle a_j \rangle_B$,
$0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (a_j)$,
and $\langle a_j \rangle_A \leq \langle b_j \rangle_A$ because $\langle a_j \rangle_B \leq \langle b_j \rangle_B$.

Passing to~$A$, we have
$\langle z \rangle_A <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle a_j \rangle_A$ for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$.
Since $A$ has $m$-comparison,
it follows that
\[
\langle z \rangle_A \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle a_j \rangle_A.
\]
Since $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (z)$
and of ${\operatorname{sp}} (a_j)$ for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$,
Theorem~\ref{T_6318_Isom_CuPl} implies that
\[
\langle z \rangle_B \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle a_j \rangle_B.
\]
Therefore
\[
\langle (y - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_B
 \leq \langle z \rangle_B
 \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle a_j \rangle_B
 \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle b_j \rangle_B.
\]

We have proved that for all $\varepsilon > 0$ we have
\[
\langle (y - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_B \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle b_j \rangle_B.
\]
So $\langle y \rangle_B \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle b_j \rangle_B$.
This completes the case where $\langle y \rangle_B$ is not the class of a projection.

Now assume that $\langle y \rangle_B$ is the class of a nonzero
projection in $B \otimes K$.
Use Lemma 2.6 of \cite{Phl40} to choose
$b \in (B \otimes K)_{+} \setminus \{0\}$ such that
$\langle b \rangle_B \leq \langle b_j \rangle_B$ for $j = 0, 1, \hdots, m$.
Set $k = \max ( k_0, k_1, \ldots, k_m )$.
By Lemma~\ref{L_3618_Lg36}, there exist $\mu, \eta \in {\operatorname{Cu}}_{+} (B)$
such that
\[
\mu \leq \langle y \rangle_B \leq \mu + \eta
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
(2 k + 2) \eta \leq \langle b \rangle_B.
\]
For $j = 0, 1, \hdots, m$, we then have
\[
\begin{split}
(2 k_j + 2) (\mu + \eta)
& \leq (2 k_j + 2) \langle y \rangle_B + (2 k_j + 2) \eta
\\
& \leq 2 k_j \langle b_j \rangle_B + \langle b \rangle_B
  \leq 2 k_j \langle b_j \rangle_B + \langle b_j \rangle_B
  = (2 k_j + 1) \langle b_j \rangle_B.
\end{split}
\]
Hence $\mu + \eta <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle b_j \rangle_B$.
Since $\mu + \eta \in {\operatorname{Cu}}_{+} (B)$, the previous case yields
the second step in the calculation
\[
\langle y \rangle_B \leq \mu + \eta \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle a_j \rangle_B.
\]
This completes the proof that if ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ has
$m$-comparison, then so does ${\operatorname{Cu}} (B)$.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lem-6: body_md -->
\label{L_5Z29_CC_down}
Let $A$ be an infinite dimensional simple stably finite unital C*-algebra,
and let $B \subseteq A$ be a subalgebra
which is large in the sense of Definition~4.1 of~\cite{Phl40}.
Let $\iota \colon B \to A$ be the inclusion map.
Let $\xi, \eta \in {\operatorname{Cu}} (B)$ satisfy $\iota_* (\xi) \ll \iota_* (\eta)$.
Then $\xi \ll \eta$.
<!-- end lem-6 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
Choose $x, y \in B \otimes K$ such that such that
$\langle x \rangle_B = \xi$ and $\langle y \rangle_B = \eta$.
If there is a
projection{} $p \in B$ such that $\xi = \langle p \rangle_B$, then
\[
\xi = \langle p \rangle_B \ll \langle p \rangle_B = \xi \leq \eta,
\]
which is the desired conclusion.
The conclusion follows the
same way if $\eta$ is the class of a projection.
Therefore we may
assume that $0$ is a limit point of both ${\operatorname{sp}} (x)$ and
${\operatorname{sp}} (y)$.

Since $\langle x \rangle_A \ll \langle y \rangle_A$, Lemma~\ref{L_5Z28_CptCont}
provides $\varepsilon > 0$ such that $x \precsim_A (y - \varepsilon)_{+}$.
Since $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (y)$, there is
$\rho \in (0, \varepsilon)$ such that
${\operatorname{sp}} (y) \cap (\rho, \varepsilon) \neq \varnothing$.
Choose
$\lambda \in {\operatorname{sp}} (y) \cap (\rho, \varepsilon)$.
Choose a continuous function{} $f \colon [0, \infty) \to [0, 1]$ such that
${\operatorname{supp}} (f) \subseteq (\rho, \varepsilon)$ and $f (\lambda) \neq 0$.
Use Lemma~\ref{L_3618_LgSp01} to choose
$c \in \bigl( {\overline{f (y) (B \otimes K) f (y)}} \bigr)_{+}$
such that ${\operatorname{sp}} (c) = [0, 1]$.
Then
\[
(y - \varepsilon)_{+} \leq (y - \varepsilon)_{+} + c \precsim_B (y - \rho)_{+}.
\]
It follows that
\[
x \precsim_A (y - \varepsilon)_{+} \leq (y - \varepsilon)_{+} + c.
\]
Since $0$ is a limit point of both ${\operatorname{sp}} (x)$
and ${\operatorname{sp}} \bigl( (y - \varepsilon)_{+} + c \bigr)$,
Theorem~\ref{T_6318_Isom_CuPl} implies that
$x \precsim_B (y - \varepsilon)_{+} + c$.
Combining this with
$(y - \varepsilon)_{+} + c \precsim_B (y - \rho)_{+}$,
we get $x \precsim_B (y - \rho)_{+}$.
So
$\langle x \rangle_B \ll \langle y \rangle_B$ by
Lemma~\ref{L_5Z28_CptCont}.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- prp-3: body_md -->
\label{P_5Z28_Large_alm_dv}
Let $A$ be an infinite dimensional simple stably finite unital C*-algebra,
and let $B \subseteq A$ be a subalgebra which is large in the sense
of Definition~4.1 of~\cite{Phl40}.
Let $m \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}$.
Suppose that $B$ is $m$-almost divisible.
Then $A$ is $(2 m + 1)$-almost divisible.
<!-- end prp-3 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
Let $\iota \colon B \to A$ be the inclusion map.
Let $k \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$.
Let $\xi, \eta \in {\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ satisfy $\xi \ll \eta$.
Choose $a, b \in (A \otimes K)_{+}$ such that
$\langle a \rangle_A = \xi$ and $\langle b \rangle_A = \eta$.

First suppose that $a = 0$ or $0$ is a limit point of
${\operatorname{sp}} (a)$, and that $b = 0$ or $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (b)$.
We claim that in this case, we
actually get $\gamma \in {\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_5Z28_Case_1}
k \gamma \leq \eta
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
\xi \leq (k + 1) (m + 1) \gamma.
\end{equation}
 
This claim certainly implies the desired conclusion for
this case, which has merely $k \gamma \leq \eta$ and
$\xi \leq (k + 1) (2 m + 2) \gamma$.

To prove the claim, use Corollary~\ref{C_6318_LgCmp}
to find $\xi_0, \eta_0 \in {\operatorname{Cu}} (B)$
such that $\iota_* (\xi_0) = \xi$ and $\iota_* (\eta_0) = \eta$.
Then $\xi_0 \ll \eta_0$ by Lemma~\ref{L_5Z29_CC_down}.
So the hypothesis provides $\gamma_0 \in {\operatorname{Cu}} (B)$
such that $k \gamma_0 \leq \eta_0$ and
$\xi_0 \leq (k + 1) (m + 1) \gamma_0$.
Set $\gamma = \iota_* (\gamma_0)$.
Then~(\ref{Eq_5Z28_Case_1}) holds, as claimed.

Otherwise, one or both of $a$ and $b$ is nonzero but
its spectrum does not have $0$ as a limit point.
Therefore there is a nonzero projection{} $p \in A \otimes K$
such that $\langle p \rangle_A = \langle a \rangle_A$ or there is a nonzero projection{}
$p \in A \otimes K$ such that $\langle p \rangle_A = \langle b \rangle_A$.
In either case, it suffices to prove the desired
conclusion when $a = b = p$.

The case $N = 1$ of Lemma~\ref{L_5Z26_InterpPj} provides
$x, y \in (p (A \otimes K) p)_{+}$ and $\varepsilon \in (0, 1)$
such that
\[
{\operatorname{sp}} (x) = {\operatorname{sp}} (y) = [0, 1],
\quad
x \precsim_A p,
\quad
p \precsim_A (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \oplus (y - \varepsilon)_{+},
\quad {\mbox{and}} \quad
y \precsim_A (x - \varepsilon)_{+}.
\]
Lemma~\ref{L_5Z28_CptCont} tells us that
$\langle (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_A \ll \langle x \rangle_A$.
Since $\varepsilon < 1$,
it follows that $0$ is a limit point of
${\operatorname{sp}} ((x - \varepsilon)_{+})$ as well as of ${\operatorname{sp}} (x)$.
By the claim above (see~(\ref{Eq_5Z28_Case_1})),
there is $\gamma \in {\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$ such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_5Z28_xme_x}
k \gamma \leq \langle x \rangle_A
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
\langle (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_A \leq (k + 1) (m + 1) \gamma.
\end{equation}
 
Now
\[
k \gamma \leq \langle x \rangle_A \leq \langle p \rangle_A
\]
and, using $y \precsim_A (x - \varepsilon)_{+}$ at the second
step and the second part of~(\ref{Eq_5Z28_xme_x}) at the
third step,
\[
\langle p \rangle_A \leq \langle (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_A +
\langle (y - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_A
\leq 2 \langle (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_A
\leq (k + 1) (2 m + 2) \gamma.
\]
This completes the proof of the proposition.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- prp-4: body_md -->
\label{P_5Z28_Large_alm_dv_C}
Let $A$ be an infinite dimensional simple stably finite unital C*-algebra,
and let $B \subseteq A$ be a subalgebra which is large in the sense
of Definition~4.1 of~\cite{Phl40}.
Let $m \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}$.
Suppose that $A$ is $m$-almost divisible.
Then $B$ is $(4 m + 3)$-almost divisible.
<!-- end prp-4 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
The proof of this proposition is similar to
that of Proposition~\ref{P_5Z28_Large_alm_dv}.
Let $k \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$ and let $\xi, \eta \in {\operatorname{Cu}} (B)$ satisfy
$\xi \ll \eta$.
Choose $a, b \in (B \otimes K)_{+}$
such that $\langle a \rangle_B = \xi$ and $\langle b \rangle_B = \eta$.

First suppose that $a = 0$ or $0$ is a limit point of
${\operatorname{sp}} (a)$, and that $b = 0$ or $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (b)$.
In this case, we will prove the following stronger conclusion:
for every $k \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$ there is $\gamma \in {\operatorname{Cu}} (B)$ such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6318_Strong}
k \gamma \leq \langle b \rangle_B \quad \text{and} \quad
\langle a \rangle_B \leq (k + 1) (2 m + 2) \gamma.
\end{equation}
 
Since $\langle a \rangle_B \ll \langle b \rangle_B$ and homomorphism{s} preserve the
relation $\ll$ (this is obvious from Lemma~\ref{L_5Z28_CptCont}),
we have $\langle a \rangle_A \ll \langle b \rangle_A$ in ${\operatorname{Cu}} (A)$.
Using $m$-almost divisibility of $A$, we obtain
$c\in (A \otimes K)_{+}$ such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_A_div}
2 k \langle c \rangle_A \leq \langle b \rangle_A \quad \text{and} \quad
\langle a \rangle_A \leq (2 k + 1) (m + 1) \langle c \rangle_A.
\end{equation}

Suppose that $\langle c \rangle_A \in {\operatorname{Cu}}_{+}(A) \cup \left\{ 0 \right\}$,
so that $2 \langle c \rangle_A \in {\operatorname{Cu}}_{+} (A) \cup \left\{ 0 \right\}$.
By Corollary~\ref{C_6318_LgCmp}, there is $d \in (B \otimes K)_{+}$
such that $\langle d \rangle_A = \langle c \rangle_A$ and $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (d)$.
Using (\ref{Eq_A_div}), we have
 
\[
k \langle d \rangle_A \leq 2 k \langle d \rangle_A \leq \langle b \rangle_A
\]
and
\[
\langle a \rangle_A \leq (2 k + 1) (m + 1) \langle d \rangle_A
         \leq (k + 1) (2 m + 2) \langle d \rangle_A.
\]
 
By Theorem~\ref{T_6318_Isom_CuPl}, these relations
also hold for the classes in ${\operatorname{Cu}} (B)$.
This is (\ref{Eq_6318_Strong}) with $\gamma = \langle d \rangle_B$.

Let us now suppose that $\langle c \rangle_A$ is a class of nonzero projection.
By Lemma~\ref{L_3618_Lg36}, there exist
$\mu, \kappa \in {\operatorname{Cu}}_{+} (A)$ such that
\[
\mu \leq \langle c \rangle_A \leq \mu + \kappa \quad \text{and}
\quad \kappa \leq \langle c \rangle_A.
\]
Then $\mu + \kappa \in {\operatorname{Cu}}_{+} (A)$, and from (\ref{Eq_A_div})
we see that
 
\[
k (\mu + \kappa) \leq 2 k \langle c \rangle_A \leq \langle b \rangle_A \quad
\text{and} \quad \langle a \rangle_A \leq (k + 1) (2 m +2) (\mu + \kappa).
\]
 
Corollary~\ref{C_6318_LgCmp} provides
$d \in (B \otimes K)_{+}$ such that $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}}(d)$
and $\langle d \rangle_A = \mu + \kappa$, and Theorem~\ref{T_6318_Isom_CuPl} implies that
\[
k \langle d \rangle_B \leq \langle b \rangle_B
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
\langle a \rangle_B \leq (k + 1) (2 m + 2) \langle d \rangle_B.
\]
This is (\ref{Eq_6318_Strong}) with $\gamma = \langle d \rangle_B$.

It remains to consider the case in which
one or both of $a$ and $b$ is nonzero but
its spectrum does not have $0$ as a limit point.
In this case, there is a nonzero projection{} $p \in B \otimes K$
such that $\langle p \rangle_B = \langle a \rangle_B$ or there is a nonzero projection{}
$p \in B \otimes K$ such that $\langle p \rangle_B = \langle b \rangle_B$.
In either case, $\langle a \rangle_B \leq \langle p \rangle_B \ll \langle p \rangle_B \leq \langle b \rangle_B$,
and it suffices to prove the desired
conclusion when $a = b = p$.
By Lemma~\ref{L_5Z26_InterpPj}, there exist
$x, y \in (p (B \otimes K) p)_{+}$ and $\varepsilon \in (0, 1)$
such that
\[
{\operatorname{sp}} (x) = {\operatorname{sp}} (y) = [0, 1], \quad x \precsim_B p, \quad
p \precsim_B (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \oplus (y - \varepsilon)_{+},
\quad {\mbox{and}}
\quad y \precsim_B (x - \varepsilon)_{+}.
\]
Since $\langle (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_B \ll \langle x \rangle_B$ and $0$ is a
limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} ((x - \varepsilon)_{+})$ as well as of
${\operatorname{sp}} (x)$, by the previous case (see~(\ref{Eq_6318_Strong})),
we obtain $\gamma \in {\operatorname{Cu}} (B)$ such that
 
\begin{equation*}
k \gamma \leq \langle x \rangle_B \leq \langle p \rangle_B
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
\langle (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_B \leq (k + 1) (2 m + 2) \gamma.
\end{equation*}
 
Now
\[
\langle p \rangle_B \leq 2 \langle (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_B
\leq (k + 1) (4 m + 4) \gamma.
\]
Thus $B$ is $(4m + 3)$-almost divisible.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- thm-4: body_md -->
\label{T_LargePure}
Let $A$ be an infinite dimensional simple stably finite unital C*-algebra,
and let $B \subseteq A$ be a subalgebra which is large in the sense
of Definition~4.1 of~\cite{Phl40}.
Then $A$ is pure if and
only if $B$ is pure.
<!-- end thm-4 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
If $B$ is pure, then $B$ has $0$-comparison and is $0$-almost
divisible.
So $A$ has $0$-comparison by
Theorem~\ref{T_6314_CompThm} and is $1$-divisible by
Proposition~\ref{P_5Z28_Large_alm_dv}.
Together, these imply $A$ is pure by Theorem~\ref{T_PureEquiv}.

Conversely, suppose that $A$ is pure.
Then $A$ has
$0$-comparison and is $0$-almost divisible.
So $B$ has $0$-comparison by Theorem~\ref{T_6314_CompThm}
and is $3$-almost divisible by Proposition~\ref{P_5Z28_Large_alm_dv_C}.
Together, these imply $B$ is pure by Theorem~\ref{T_PureEquiv}.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- ntn-2: body_md -->
\label{N_4Y12_C0UD}
For a locally compact Hausdorff space~$X$
and a C*-algebra~$D$,
we identify $C_0 (X, D) = C_0 (X) \otimes D$
in the standard way.
For an open subset $U \subseteq X$,
we use the abbreviation
\[
C_0 (U, D)
= \big\{ a \in C_0 (X, D) \colon
{\mbox{$a (x) = 0$ for all $x \in X \setminus U$}} \big\}
\subseteq C_0 (X, D).
\]
This subalgebra is of course canonically isomorphic to
the usual algebra $C_0 (U, D)$ when $U$ is considered
as a locally compact Hausdorff space{} in its own right.
<!-- end ntn-2 -->
<!-- lem-7: body_md -->
\label{L_1_GetAuto}
Let $X$ be a locally compact Hausdorff space, let $h \colon X \to X$ be a homeomorphism,
and let $D$ be a C*-algebra.
Then there is a one to one correspondence
between actions of ${\mathbb{Z}}$ on $C_0 (X, D)$ that lie over $h$ and
continuous functions from $X$ to ${\operatorname{Aut}} (D)$, given as follows.

For any function $x \mapsto \alpha_{x}$ from $X$ to ${\operatorname{Aut}} (D)$
such that $x \mapsto \alpha_{x} (d)$ is continuous{} for all $d \in D$,
there is an automorphism $\alpha \in {\operatorname{Aut}} ( C_0 (X, D))$
given by $\alpha (a) (x) = \alpha_x (a (h^{-1} (x)))$ for all
$a \in C_0 (X, D)$ and $x \in X$, and this automorphism lies
over~$h$.

Conversely, if $\alpha \in {\operatorname{Aut}} ( C_0 (X, D))$ lies over~$h$,
then there is a function $x \mapsto \alpha_{x}$ from $X$ to
${\operatorname{Aut}} (D)$ such that $x \mapsto \alpha_{x} (d)$ is continuous{} for
all $d \in D$ and such that
$\alpha (a) (x) = \alpha_x (a (h^{-1} (x)))$ for all
$a \in C_0 (X, D)$ and $x \in X$.
<!-- end lem-7 -->
<!-- dfn-10: body_md -->
\label{D_1_OrbSubalg}
Let $X$ be a locally compact Hausdorff space,
let $h \colon X \to X$ be a homeomorphism,
let $D$ be a C*-algebra,
and let $\alpha \in {\operatorname{Aut}} ( C_0 (X, D))$
be an automorphism which lies over~$h$.
Let $u$ be
the standard unitary implementing the action of
$\alpha$ in the crossed product C*-algebra
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C_0 (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$.
Let $Y \subseteq X$ be a nonempty closed subset,
and, following~(\ref{Eq_4Y12_C0XYD}) in
Notation~\ref{N_4Y12_C0UD}, define the
corresponding {\emph{orbit breaking subalgebra}} of
$C^* \big( {\mathbb{Z}},  C_0 (X, D), \alpha \big)$ to be
\[
C^* \big( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C_0 (X, D), \, \alpha \big)_Y
= C^* \big(  C_0 (X, D), \, C_0 (X \setminus Y, \, D) u \big)
\subseteq C^* \big( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C_0 (X, D), \, \alpha \big).
\]
<!-- end dfn-10 -->
<!-- lem-8: body_md -->
\label{L_2_OrbBreakPure}
Let $X$ be a compact metric space, let $h \colon X \to X$ be a
minimal homeomorphism, let $D$ be a simple unital pure C*-algebra,
and let $\alpha \in {\operatorname{Aut}} (C (X,D))$ lie over $h$.
Choose any nonempty
closed set $Y \subseteq X$ such that ${\operatorname{int}}(Y) \neq \varnothing$.
Then
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)_{Y}$ is pure.
<!-- end lem-8 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
By Theorem~3.17 of~\cite{ArBcPh2}, the algebra
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)_{Y}$ is a
recursive subhomogeneous algebra over $D$ in the sense of
Definition~3.2 of~\cite{ArBcPh2}, and so is pure by
Proposition~6.5 of~\cite{SethVil}.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- prp-5: body_md -->
\label{P_3_ApproxSubPure}
Let $X$ be a compact metric space, let $h \colon X \to X$ be a
minimal homeomorphism, let $D$ be a simple unital pure C*-algebra,
and let $\alpha \in {\operatorname{Aut}} (C (X,D))$ lie over $h$.
Choose any nonempty
closed set $Y \subseteq X$ such that $h^{n} (Y) \cap Y = \varnothing$
for all $n \in {\mathbb{Z}} \setminus \left\{ 0 \right\}$.
Then
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)_{Y}$ is pure.
<!-- end prp-5 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
For such a choice of $Y$, the algebra
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)_{Y}$ is a
direct limit (with injective maps) of recursive subhomogeneous
algebras over $D$ (of the form given in
Lemma~\ref{L_2_OrbBreakPure}) by
Proposition~4.2 of~\cite{ArBcPh2}.
Pureness follows by
Theorem~3.8 of~\cite{PerThiVil}.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- thm-5: body_md -->
\label{T_4_PureCrossProd}
Let $X$ be a compact metric space, let $h \colon X \to X$ be a
minimal homeomorphism, let $D$ be a simple unital pure C*-algebra,
and let $\alpha \in {\operatorname{Aut}} (C (X,D))$ lie over $h$.
Then the crossed
product $C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$ is pure.
<!-- end thm-5 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
Choose any point $x \in X$ and set $Y = \left\{ x \right\}$.
Then
the minimality of $h$ implies that $h^{n}(Y) \cap Y =
\varnothing$ for $n \in {\mathbb{Z}} \setminus \left\{ 0 \right\}$, and so
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)_{Y}$ is pure
by Proposition~\ref{P_3_ApproxSubPure}.

Since $D$ is simple and pure, it has strict comparison
of positive elements, and so Corollary~2.12(2)
of~\cite{ArBcPh2} implies that
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)_{Y}$
is a centrally large subalgebra of
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$.
Pureness now follows from Theorem~\ref{T_LargePure}.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- cor-2: body_md -->
\label{C_5_CrossProdSR1}
Let $X$ be a compact metric space, let $h \colon X \to X$ be a
minimal homeomorphism, let $D$ be a simple unital pure C*-algebra,
and let $\alpha \in {\operatorname{Aut}} (C (X,D))$ lie over $h$.
Then the crossed
product $C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$ has stable
rank one.
<!-- end cor-2 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
This follows immediately from Theorem~\ref{T_4_PureCrossProd}
and Corollary~1.3 of~\cite{HLin}.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- cor-3: body_md -->
\label{C_6_CrossProdRR0}
Let $X$ be a compact metric space, let $h \colon X \to X$ be a
minimal homeomorphism, let $D$ be a simple unital pure exact
C*-algebra, and let $\alpha \in {\operatorname{Aut}} (C (X,D))$ lie over $h$.
Then the crossed product
$A = C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$
has real rank zero if and only if the canonical map
$\rho \colon K_{0}(A) \to {\operatorname{Aff}} (T(A))$ has dense range.
<!-- end cor-3 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
Theorem~\ref{T_4_PureCrossProd} implies that
$A$ is pure. It follows that $A$ has stable rank one by
Corollary~\ref{C_5_CrossProdSR1},
and that ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ is almost unperforated
by Proposition~\ref{CP_6325_WA_Cmp}.
The exactness of $D$ implies that $A$ is also exact.
Now apply Proposition 7.1 of~\cite{Rord2004} to
obtain the given conclusion.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- lem-9: body_md -->
\label{L_1_TracesTensorProd}
Let $A$ and $B$ be simple unital C*-algebras.
Assume $B$ has a unique tracial state $\sigma$.
Then the map $\rho \mapsto \rho \otimes \sigma$
is a bijection from $T(A)$ to $T(A \otimes_{\min} B)$.
<!-- end lem-9 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
Let $\tau$ be a tracial state on $A \otimes_{\min} B$.
For fixed $a \in A_{+}$, define a map $\omega_{a}$ on
$B$ by $\omega_{a} (b) = \tau (a \otimes b)$.
Then
$\omega_{a}$ is a bounded linear functional on $B$,
and is positive.
For any $b,c \in B$, we have
 
\begin{align*}
\omega_{a} (bc) - \omega_{a}(cb) &= \tau (a \otimes bc) -
\tau (a \otimes cb) \\
&= \tau (a \otimes bc - a \otimes cb) \\
&= \tau ( (a \otimes b) (1_{A} \otimes c) -
(1_{A} \otimes c) (a \otimes b) ) = 0
\end{align*}
 
and so $\omega_{a}$ has the trace property.
Therefore,
it is a nonnegative multiple of $\sigma$, say
$\omega_{a} (b) = \rho (a) \sigma (b)$.
Writing
$a \in A$ as a linear combination of positive
elements allows the extension of $\rho$ to a map
$A \to {\mathbb{C}}$.
Positivity and the condition
that $\rho (1) = 1$ are immediate.
The trace
property of $\rho$ is easily verified via the
calculation
 
\[
\rho (xy) = \rho(xy) \sigma (1_{B}) = \omega_{xy}(1_{B})
= \tau (xy \otimes 1_{B}) = \tau (yx \otimes 1_{B})
= \rho (yx) \sigma(1_{B}) = \rho (yx).
\]
 
Additivity of $\rho$ on $A_{+}$ follows from the
observation (using the linearity of $\tau$) that
 
\begin{align*}
\rho (a_{1} + a_{2}) \sigma (b)
= \omega_{a_{1} + a_{2}} (b)
&= \tau ((a_{1} + a_{2}) \otimes b) \\
&= \tau (a_{1} \otimes b) + \tau (a_{2} \otimes b)
= \rho (a_{1}) \sigma (b) + \rho (a_{2}) \sigma (b)
\end{align*}
 
for any $a_{1}, a_{2} \in A_{+}$, and linearity
of $\rho$ follows.
Hence $\rho$ is a tracial state
on A.
That $\rho \otimes \sigma = \tau$ follows from
density of the linear span of the elementary tensors.
This gives surjectivity of the map
$\rho \mapsto \rho \otimes \sigma$,
and injectivity follows easily by evaluation on
$a \otimes 1_{B}$.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- lem-10: body_md -->
\label{L_2_AveApprox}
Let $D$ be a simple unital C*-algebra, let $X$ be
a compact metric space, let $h \colon X \to X$ be
a minimal homeomorphism, and obtain an action
$\alpha \colon X \to {\operatorname{Aut}} (D)$ as in
Lemma~\ref{L_1_GetAuto}.
For every $a \in
C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$ and every
$\varepsilon > 0$, there exist an $n \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$ and
$s_1 , \ldots , s_n \in C (X,D)$ such that
$\lVert s_k (x) \rVert = 1$ for all $x \in X$, and
 
\[
\Big\lVert E(a) - \frac{1}{n}
\sum_{k=1}^{n} s_k a s_k^* \Big\rVert < \varepsilon.
\]
<!-- end lem-10 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
Choose $N \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}$ and $f_k \in C (X,D)$ for
$-N \leq k \leq N$ such that
$b = \sum_{k = -N}^{N} f_k u^k$ satisfies
$\lVert a - b \rVert < \varepsilon/2$.
Set
$F = \left\{ -N, \ldots,N \right\}$.
By Proposition 11.1.19
of~\cite{GKPT}, there exist $n \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$ and
$t_1 , \ldots , t_n \in C (X)$ such that,,
for $1 \leq k \leq n$,
$\vert t_k (x) \vert = 1$ for all $x \in X$
and such that, for all
$x \in X$ and $j \in F \setminus {0}$, we have
 
\[
\frac{1}{n} \sum_{k=1}^{n} t_k (x)
\overline{ t_k (h^{-j}(x)) } = 0.
\]
 
Set $s_k = t_k \otimes 1_D$.
Define
$P \colon C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)
\to C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$ by
 
\[
P(c) = \frac{1}{n} \sum_{k=1}^{n} s_k c s_k^*.
\]
 
We first compute
 
\begin{align*}
P(f_0) = \frac{1}{n} \sum_{k=1}^{n} s_k f_0 s_k^*
&= \frac{1}{n} \sum_{k=1}^{n} (t_k \otimes 1_D)
f_0 (t_k \otimes 1_D)^* \\ &= f_0 \cdot \frac{1}{n}
\sum_{k=1}^{n} (t_k \otimes 1_D) (t_k \otimes 1_D)^*
= f_0 = E(f_0).
\end{align*}
 
It is easy to show that, for $j \neq 0$ and any
$x \in X$, we have
 
\[
\alpha^j (s_k^*) (x) = \alpha^j (t_k^* \otimes 1_D) (x)
= \overline{ t_k (h^{-j}(x)) } \cdot 1_D,
\]
 
and hence, for any $x \in X$,
\[
\frac{1}{n} \sum_{k=1}^{n} s_k (x) \alpha^j (s_k^*) (x)
= \Big[ \frac{1}{n} \sum_{k=1}^{n} t_k (x)
\overline{ t_k (h^{-j}(x)) } \Big] \cdot 1_D = 0.
\]
 
It follows that, for any $j \in F \setminus \left\{ 0 \right\}$,
 
\[
P(f_j u^j) =
\frac{1}{n} \sum_{k=1}^{n} s_k f_j u^j s_k^*
= \frac{1}{n} \sum_{k=1}^{n} f_j s_k u^j s_k^*
u^{-j} u^j = f_j \Big[
\frac{1}{n} \sum_{k=1}^{n} s_k \alpha^j (s_k^*) \Big] u^j
= 0.
\]
 
This implies that $P(b) = E(b)$, and observing that
$\lVert s_k \rVert = 1$ for $1 \leq k \leq n$, we
obtain $\lVert P \rVert \leq 1$.
Now,
 
\begin{align*}
\lVert E(a) - P(a) \rVert &= \lVert E(a) - E(b) \rVert
+ \lVert E(b) - P(b) \rVert + \lVert P(b) - P(a) \rVert \\
&< \varepsilon/2 + 0 + \varepsilon/2 \\
&= \varepsilon.
\end{align*}
 
This is the desired result.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- prp-6: body_md -->
\label{P_1_TracialStates}
Let $D$ be a simple unital exact C*-algebra with
a unique tracial state $\sigma$, let $X$ be a compact
metric space, let $h \colon X \to X$ be a minimal
homeomorphism, and obtain an action
$\alpha \colon X \to {\operatorname{Aut}} (D)$ as in
Lemma~\ref{L_1_GetAuto}.
For an $h$-invariant Borel probability
measure $\mu$ on $X$, define a functional $\tau_{\mu}$
on $C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$ by
 
\[
\tau_{\mu}(a) = \int_{X} \sigma (a (x)) \, d \mu (x).
\]
 
Then $\tau_{\mu}$ is always a tracial state, and
$\mu \mapsto \tau_{\mu}$ is an affine bijective
homeomorphism from the space $M_{h}(X)$ of $h$-invariant
Borel probability measures on $X$ to the tracial states
on $C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$.
<!-- end prp-6 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
For an invariant probability measure $\mu$, define
a tracial state $\rho_{\mu}$ on $C (X, D)$ by the formula
 
\[
\rho_{\mu} (f) = \int_X \sigma (f (x)) d \mu (x).
\]
 
Obviously $\rho_{\mu}$ is a tracial state,
and it is $\alpha$-invariant because $\sigma$ is invariant
under all automorphisms of $D$.
Lemma~\ref{L_1_TracesTensorProd} implies that these
are all the $\alpha$-invariant tracial states on $C (X, D)$.

Let
$E \colon C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr) \to C (X, D)$
be the standard conditional expectation.
Then for any
$\alpha$-invariant tracial state $\rho_{\mu}$ defined above,
$\tau_{\mu} = \rho_{\mu} \circ E$ defines a tracial state
on $C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$.
Now let
$\tau$ be any tracial state on
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$.
Restricting
$\tau$ to $C (X, D)$ gives an $\alpha$-invariant tracial
state on $C (X, D)$, and so there is an invariant
measure $\mu$ such that $\tau \vert_{C (X,D)} = \rho_{\mu}$.
We want to show that $\tau = \tau_{\mu}$.
Since $\tau (f)
= \tau \vert_{C (X,D)} (f) = \rho_{\mu} (f)$
for every $f \in C (X,D)$, it suffices
to prove that, for every $\varepsilon> 0$ and every $a \in
C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$, we have
$\vert \tau (a) - \tau (E(a)) \vert < \varepsilon$.
Apply
Lemma~\ref{L_2_AveApprox} to obtain $n \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$ and
$s_1 , \ldots , s_n \in C (X,D)$ such that
$\lVert s_k \rVert = 1$ for all $k$ and
 
\[
\Big\lVert E(a) - \frac{1}{n} \sum_{k=1}^{n} s_k a s_k^*
\Big\rVert < \varepsilon.
\]
 
For each $k$, the trace property gives $\tau (a) =
\tau (a s_k^* s_k ) = \tau (s_k a s_k^* )$, and hence
${\displaystyle{ \tau (a) = \tau \Big( \tfrac{1}{n}
\sum_{k=1}^{n} s_k a s_k^* \Big) }}$. It follows that
 
\[
\vert \tau (a) - \tau (E(a)) \vert = \Big\vert \tau
\Big( \tfrac{1}{n} \sum_{k=1}^{n} s_k a s_k^* \Big) -
\tau (E(a)) \Big\vert \leq \Big\lVert
\frac{1}{n} \sum_{k=1}^{n} s_k a s_k^* - E(a) \Big\rVert
< \varepsilon,
\]
 
as required.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- cor-4: body_md -->
\label{C_1_RR0_Condition}
Let $D$ be a simple unital exact C*-algebra with
a unique tracial state, let $X$ be a compact
metric space, let $h \colon X \to X$ be a minimal
homeomorphism, and obtain an action
$\alpha \colon X \to {\operatorname{Aut}} ( C (X, D))$ as in
Lemma~\ref{L_1_GetAuto}.
If the range of the
canonical map
$K_{0} ( C^* ( {\mathbb{Z}}, X, h ) ) \to
{\operatorname{Aff}} (T( C^* ( {\mathbb{Z}}, X, h ) )$
is dense, then the range of the canonical map
$K_{0} ( C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr) ) \to
{\operatorname{Aff}} (T( C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr) ))$
is dense.
<!-- end cor-4 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
Since $D$ has a unique tracial state,
Proposition~\ref{P_1_TracialStates} implies that
 
\[
T( C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr) ) \cong
T ( C^* ( {\mathbb{Z}}, X, h ) ) \cong M_{h} (X).
\]
 
Since
$C^* ( {\mathbb{Z}}, X, h )$ is a subalgebra of
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$, the result
follows.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- cor-5: body_md -->
\label{C_6327_RRZ}
Adopt the hypotheses of Corollary~\ref{C_1_RR0_Condition}.
If the range of the canonical map
$K_{0} ( C^* ( {\mathbb{Z}}, X, h ) ) \to {\operatorname{Aff}} (T ( C^* ( {\mathbb{Z}}, X, h ) )$
is dense, then
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$ has real rank zero.
<!-- end cor-5 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
Combine Corollary~\ref{C_1_RR0_Condition}
with Corollary~\ref{C_6_CrossProdRR0}.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- ntn-3: body_md -->
\label{N_6326_TrA}
Let $G$ be a compact group.
We write $\mathtt{Lt} \colon G \to {\operatorname{Aut}} (C (G))$
for the left action of $G$ on $C (G)$,
given by $\mathtt{Lt}_g (f) (t) = f (g^{-1} t)$.
<!-- end ntn-3 -->
<!-- lem-11: body_md -->
\label{D_6326_SeqSpl}
Let $G$ be a second countable compact group. Let $A$ be a separable C*-algebra,
and let $\alpha \colon G \to {\operatorname{Aut}} (A)$ be an action.
Let $d \in {\mathbb{Z}} \setminus \left\{ 0 \right\}$.
Then ${\operatorname{dim}}_{\operatorname{Rok}}^{\operatorname{c}} (\alpha) \leq d$ if and only if, for any finite sets
$F \subseteq A$ and $S \subseteq C (G)$ and any $\varepsilon > 0$,
there exist $d + 1$ completely positive contractive maps
\[
\psi_0, \psi_1, \ldots, \psi_d \colon C (G) \to A
\]
satisfying the following conditions:
\begin{enumerate}
\item
For any $a \in F$ and $f\in S$,
$\|[\psi_j(f), a]\| < \varepsilon$ for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, d$.
\item
For any $f\in S$ and $g\in G$,
$\|\alpha_g (\psi_j(f)) - \psi_j(\mathtt{Lt}_g (f)) \| < \varepsilon$
for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, d$
\item
$\|\psi_j(f_1) \psi_j(f_2) \| < \varepsilon$
whenever $f_1$ and $f_2$ in $S$ are orthogonal.
\item
For any $a \in F$, $\|\sum_{j=0}^d \psi_j(1_{C (G)})a - a\| < \varepsilon$.
\item
For any $f_1,f_2 \in S$,
$\|[\psi_j(f_1), \psi_k(f_2)]\| < \varepsilon$ for $j, k = 0, 1, \ldots, d$.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-11 -->
<!-- prp-7: body_md -->
\label{2003_structure_cx}
Let $G$ be a second countable compact group,
let $X$ be a compact Hausdorff space,
and let $A$ be a separable C*-algebra.
Let $G \curvearrowright X$ be a free continuous action of $G$ on~$X$,
and let $\alpha \colon G \to {\operatorname{Aut}} (A)$ be an action of $G$ on~$A$.
Equip the C*-algebra $C (X, A)$ with the diagonal action of $G$,
denoted by $\gamma$.
Then the crossed product C*-algebra
$C^* \bigl( G, \, C(X,A), \, \gamma \bigr)$
is a continuous $C (X/G)$-algebra,
each of whose fibers is isomorphic to $A \otimes K(L^2 (G))$.
If $G$ is furthermore a Lie group,
then $C^* \bigl( G, \, C(X,A), \, \gamma \bigr)$ is the
section algebra of a locally trivial bundle over $X/G$
with fibers isomorphic to $A \otimes K(L^2 (G))$.
<!-- end prp-7 -->
<!-- dfn-11: body_md -->
Let $A$ and $B$ be two C*-algebras. A homomorphism
$\varphi\colon A \rightarrow B$ is \emph{sequentially split},
if there exists a commutative diagram of homomorphisms
of the form
\[
\begin{tikzcd}
A \arrow[rr, "\iota"] \arrow[dr, "\varphi"'] & & A_\infty\\
& B \arrow[ur, "\psi" ' ] &
\end{tikzcd}
\]
where the horizontal map $\iota$ is the canonical inclusion.
<!-- end dfn-11 -->
<!-- thm-6: body_md -->
\label{2003_rokhlin_dimension}
Let $\alpha \colon G \to {\operatorname{Aut}} (A)$ be an action
of a second countable compact group on a separable C*-algebra
such that ${\operatorname{dim}}_{\operatorname{Rok}}^{\operatorname{c}} (\alpha) < \infty$.
Then there exist a compact metric space $X$
and a free action $G \curvearrowright X$ such that, by endowing $C (X, A)$ with the diagonal $G$-action $\gamma$, the
canonical embedding
\[
\rho \colon C^* \bigl( G, \, A, \, \alpha \bigr) \to
C^* \bigl( G, \, C(X,A), \, \gamma \bigr)
\]
is sequentially split.
Furthermore, if $G$ is finite dimensional,
then $X$ may be chosen to be finite dimensional.
<!-- end thm-6 -->
<!-- prp-8: body_md -->
\label{0704_seq_split_comparison_divisibility}
Let $A$  and $B$ be separable C*-algebras, and
$\varphi \colon A\rightarrow B$ be a sequentially split homomorphism. If $B$ is pure, then so is $A$.
<!-- end prp-8 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
First observe that if $\varphi \colon A \to B$ is a sequentially split homomorphism, then the induced (amplified) homomorphism
\[
\tilde{\varphi} \colon A \otimes K \to B \otimes K
\]
is again sequentially split. By a mild abuse of notation, we continue to denote this amplified map by $\varphi$. We thus obtain a commutative diagram
\[ \begin{tikzcd} A \otimes K \arrow[rr, "\iota"] \arrow[dr, "\varphi"'] & & (A \otimes K )_\infty\\ & B \otimes K \arrow[ur, "\psi" ' ] & \end{tikzcd} \]
where $\iota$ denotes the canonical inclusion of $A \otimes K$ into $(A \otimes K)_\infty$ as constant sequences.

Since $B$ is pure, ${\operatorname{Cu}}(B)$ has $0$-almost comparison and is $0$-almost divisible. Moreover, ${\operatorname{Cu}}(A)$ has $0$-almost comparison by Theorem~2.9 of~\cite{barlak}. It remains to show that ${\operatorname{Cu}}(A)$ is $0$-almost divisible. For this, let
$\varepsilon > 0$, $k\in {\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}$, and $a\in (A \otimes K)_+$. Fix
$\left( a_n\right)_{n=1}^\infty$ to be a representative
sequence of $(\psi \circ \varphi) (a)$. Since
$(\psi \circ \varphi) (a) = \iota (a)$, we can choose $N_1$,
such that for all $n \geq N_1$,
\begin{equation*}
\| a_n - a\| < \frac{\varepsilon}{4}.
\end{equation*}
Thus, for all $n \geq N_1$, we get the following subequivalences
\begin{equation}\label{0104_divisibility_seq}
\left( a_n - \frac{\varepsilon}{4}\right)_+ \precsim_A a\quad\text{and}\quad (a- \varepsilon)_+ \precsim_A \left(a_n -\frac{\varepsilon}{2}\right)_+ \precsim_A \left(a_n - \frac{7\varepsilon}{16} \right)_+.
\end{equation}
Furthermore, since ${\operatorname{Cu}}(B)$ is $0$-almost divisible, there exists $b \in (B \otimes K)_+$ such that
\begin{equation}\label{0104_divisibility_seq_split}
k \langle b \rangle_B \leq \left\langle \left(\varphi(a)-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\right)_+ \right\rangle_B\quad\text{and}\quad \left\langle \left(\varphi(a)-\frac{3\varepsilon}{8}\right)_+ \right\rangle_B \leq (k+1) \langle b \rangle_B.
\end{equation}
From the second inequality in the above equation, we obtain
\begin{equation*}
\left(\varphi(a)-\frac{3\varepsilon}{8}\right)_+\precsim_B b \otimes 1_{k+1},
\end{equation*}
and hence
\begin{equation*}
\left((\psi \circ \varphi)(a)-\frac{3\varepsilon}{8}\right)_+\precsim_{{(A \otimes K)}_\infty} \psi(b) \otimes 1_{k+1}.
\end{equation*}
Let $e \in (A \otimes K)_\infty$ be such that
\begin{equation*}
\left\|e^* \left(\psi (b) \otimes 1_{k+1}\right) e - \left((\psi \circ \varphi)(a)- \frac{3\varepsilon}{8}\right)_+\right\| < \frac{ \varepsilon}{32}.
\end{equation*}
Fix representative sequences $(e_n)_{n=1}^\infty$ and $(b_n)_{n=1}^\infty$ for $e$ and $\psi(b)$, respectively, and choose $N_2 \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}$ such that, for all $n \geq N_2$,
\begin{equation*}
\left\| e_n^* (b_n \otimes 1_{k+1} ) e_n - \left( a_n - \frac{3\varepsilon}{8}\right)_+\right\| < \frac{\varepsilon}{32}.
\end{equation*}
It follows that, for all $n \geq N_2$,
\begin{equation}\label{0104_split_1}
\left( \left(a_n -\frac{3\varepsilon}{8}\right)_+ - \frac{\varepsilon}{16} \right)_+ \precsim_A \left(e_n^* (b_n \otimes 1_{k+1}) e_n -\frac{\varepsilon}{32}\right)_+.
\end{equation}
Let $M\in {\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}$ be such that $\|e_n\|^2 \leq M$ for all $n$. By
Lemma~1.4(8) of~\cite{Phl40},
\begin{equation}\label{0104_split_7}
\left(\left(a_n -\frac{3\varepsilon}{8}\right)_+ - \frac{\varepsilon}{16}\right)_+=  \left(a_n - \frac{7\varepsilon}{16}\right)_+,
\end{equation}
and, by Lemma~1.4(6) of~\cite{Phl40} at the second step and
Lemma~1.7 of~\cite{Phl40} at the third step, we obtain
\begin{equation}\label{0104_split_2}
\begin{split}
\left(e_n^*(b_n \otimes 1_{k+1}) e_n -\frac{\varepsilon}{32}\right)_+ & =
\left(e_n^* (b_n \otimes 1_{k+1})^{1/2} (b_n \otimes 1_{k+1})^{1/2} e_n -\frac{\varepsilon}{32}\right)_+ \\
& \sim_A \left((b_n \otimes 1_{k+1})^{1/2} e_n e_n^* (b_n \otimes 1_{k+1})^{1/2} - \frac{\varepsilon}{32}\right)_+\\
&\precsim_A \left(\|e_n\|^2 (b_n \otimes 1_{k+1}) -\frac{\varepsilon}{32}\right)_+\\
& \precsim_A \left(b_n \otimes 1_{k+1}- \frac{\varepsilon}{32 M}\right)_+.
\end{split}
\end{equation}
From \eqref{0104_split_1}, \eqref{0104_split_7}, and \eqref{0104_split_2}, we see that
\begin{equation}\label{0104_split_div_second}
\left(a_n - \frac{7\varepsilon}{16}\right)_+ \precsim_A \left(b_n \otimes 1_{k+1} - \frac{\varepsilon}{32 M}\right)_+.
\end{equation}
Moreover, the first inequality in \eqref{0104_divisibility_seq_split} implies that
\begin{equation*}
\psi(b) \otimes 1_{k} \precsim_{(A\otimes K)_\infty} \left( (\psi \circ \varphi) (a) -\frac{ \varepsilon}{4}\right)_+.
\end{equation*}
Choose $c\in (A\otimes K)_\infty$ such that
\begin{equation*}
\left\| c^* \left((\psi\circ \varphi)(a)-\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\right)_+ c - \psi(b) \otimes 1_k\right\| < \frac{\varepsilon}{32 M}.
\end{equation*}
Let $(c_n)_{n=1}^\infty$ be a representative sequence for $c$, and let $N_3\in {\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}$ be such that, for all $n\geq N_3$,
\begin{equation}
\left\|c_n^* \left(a_n - \frac{\varepsilon}{4}\right)_+ c_n - b_n \otimes 1_{k}\right\| <\frac{\varepsilon}{32M}.
\end{equation}
Then, for all $n\geq N_3$,
\begin{equation}\label{0104_split_div_first}
\left(b_n \otimes 1_k -\frac{\varepsilon}{32 M}\right)_+ \precsim_A \left( a_n -\frac{\varepsilon}{4}\right)_+.
\end{equation}
Finally, let $l:= \max \{N_1, N_2, N_3\}$. Using the first part of \eqref{0104_divisibility_seq} together with \eqref{0104_split_div_first}, we obtain
\[
k\left\langle \left(b_l - \frac{\varepsilon}{32 M}\right)_+ \right\rangle_A \leq \left\langle \left( a_l- \frac{\varepsilon}{4} \right)_+ \right\rangle_A \leq  \left\langle a \right\rangle_A,
\]
and by second part of (\ref{0104_divisibility_seq_split}) together with (\ref{0104_split_div_second}), we obtain
\[
\left\langle (a- \varepsilon)_+  \right\rangle_A \leq \left\langle \left(a_l -\frac{7\varepsilon}{16}\right)_+ \right\rangle_A \leq (k+1) \left\langle \left(b_l -\frac{\varepsilon}{32 M}\right)_+ \right\rangle_A,
\]
which gives $0$-almost divisibility of ${\operatorname{Cu}}(A)$.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- lem-12: body_md -->
\label{L_6327_CtFld}
Let $D$ be a simple pure C*-algebra, let $X$ be a compact Hausdorff space,
and let $A$ be the section algebra of a locally trivial continuous{} field
over~$X$ with fiber~$D$.
Then $A$ is pure.
<!-- end lem-12 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Since $A$ is the section algebra of a locally trivial continuous field over $X$ with fiber $D$, repeated application of Lemma~2.4 of~\cite{Dad2009} shows that $A$ admits the structure of a recursive subhomogeneous algebra over $D$. The usual definition of recursive subhomogeneous algebras over $D$ (Definition~3.2 of~\cite{ArBcPh2}) assumes that $D$ is unital; however, it extends in a straightforward way to the nonunital case, and the resulting algebra still admits an iterated pullback structure of the same form. Moreover, the same argument as in Proposition~6.5 of~\cite{SethVil} shows that $A$ is pure.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- thm-7: body_md -->
\label{T_6325_RDim}
Let $\alpha \colon G \to {\operatorname{Aut}} (A)$ be an action of a compact Lie group
on a simple separable C*-algebra $A$ such that
${\operatorname{dim}}_{\operatorname{Rok}}^{\operatorname{c}} (\alpha) < \infty$.
If $A$ is pure, then so is $C^* \bigl( G, \, A, \, \alpha \bigr)$.
<!-- end thm-7 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
Let $X$ be the compact metric space
obtained from Theorem~\ref{2003_rokhlin_dimension}.
By Proposition~\ref{2003_structure_cx},
$C^* \bigl( G, \, C(X,A), \, \gamma \bigr)$ is a locally trivial
continuous $C (X/G)$-algebra,
each of whose fibers is isomorphic to $A \otimes K(L^2 (G))$,
and hence is pure by Lemma~\ref{L_6327_CtFld}.
$C^* \bigl( G, \, A, \, \alpha \bigr)$ is thus  pure by
Proposition~\ref{0704_seq_split_comparison_divisibility}.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- dfn-12: body_md -->
\label{traR}
Let $A$ be an infinite dimensional simple unital C*-algebra,
and let $\alpha \colon G \to {\operatorname{Aut}} (A)$ be
an action of a second countable compact group $G$ on~$A$.
The action $\alpha$ has the
\emph{restricted tracial Rokhlin property with comparison}
if for every finite set $F \subseteq A$,
every finite set $S \subseteq C (G)$,
every $\varepsilon > 0$, every $x \in A_{+} \setminus \{ 0 \}$,
and every $y \in (A^{\alpha})_{+} \setminus \{ 0 \}$,
there exist a projection $p \in A^{\alpha}$ and a
unital completely positive map
$\varphi \colon (C (G), \mathtt{Lt}) \to (p A p, \alpha)$
such that the following hold.
 
\begin{enumerate}
 
\item\label{Item_893_FS_equi_cen_multi_approx}
$\varphi$ is an $(S, F, \varepsilon)$-approximately equivariant
central multiplicative map (Definition~1.5 of~\cite{MohPhil2021TRP}).
 
\item\label{1_pxcompactsets}
$1 - p \precsim_{A} x$.
 
\item\label{1_pycompactsets}
$1 - p \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} y$.
 
\item\label{1_ppcompactsets}
$1 - p \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} p$.
\setcounter{TmpEnumi}{\value{enumi}}
 
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end dfn-12 -->
<!-- thm-8: body_md -->
\label{ComparisonTRP}
Let $A$ be a stably finite simple separable infinite dimensional
unital C*-algebra,
let $G$ be a second countable compact group, and let
$\alpha \colon G \to {\operatorname{Aut}} (A)$ be an action
which has the restricted tracial Rokhlin property with comparison.
Let $m \in \mathbb{N}$.
Suppose $A$ has $m$-comparison.
Then $A^{\alpha}$ has $m$-comparison.
<!-- end thm-8 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
The proof is similar to the proof that $m$-comparison passes from
a simple C*-algebra{} to a large subalgebra, part of Theorem~\ref{T_6314_CompThm}.
By Proposition~\ref{CP_6325_WA_Cmp}, it suffices to prove
$m$-comparison in ${\operatorname{W}} (A^{\alpha})$.

Let $x, a_0, a_1, \ldots, a_m \in (M_{\infty} (A^{\alpha}))_{+}$ satisfy
$\langle x \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle a_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}$ for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$.
Without loss of generality{} $x \neq 0$.
By the definition of $<_{\operatorname{s}}$ (Definition~\ref{LeqS}),
for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$ there is $k_j \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$
such that $(k_j + 1) \langle x \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq k_j \langle a_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}$.
In particular, $a_j \neq 0$ for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$.

First assume $\langle x \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}$ is not the class of a projection.
Let $\varepsilon > 0$ be arbitrary.
We will find $z \in (M_{\infty} (A^{\alpha}))_{+}$
and, for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$,
we will find $b_j \in (M_{\infty} (A^{\alpha}))_{+}$
such that $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (z)$,
$(x - \varepsilon)_{+} \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} z$,
and, for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$, $\langle z \rangle_A <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle b_j \rangle_A$,
$0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (b_j)$, and $\langle b_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq \langle a_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}$.

Choose $\rho \in (0, \varepsilon)$ such that ${\operatorname{sp}} (x) \cap (\rho, \varepsilon) \neq \varnothing$.
Choose $\lambda \in {\operatorname{sp}} (x) \cap (\rho, \varepsilon)$.
Choose a continuous function{} $f \colon [0, \infty) \to [0, 1]$ such that
${\operatorname{supp}} (f) \subseteq (\rho, \varepsilon)$ and $f (\lambda) \neq 0$.
The algebra $A^{\alpha}$ is simple
by Theorem~3.1 of~\cite{MohPhil2021TRP} and not of Type~I
by Proposition~3.2 of~\cite{MohPhil2021TRP}.
Therefore Lemma~\ref{L_3618_Sp01} provides
$c \in \bigl( {\overline{f (x) (M_{\infty} (A^{\alpha})) f (x)}} \bigr)_{+}$
such that ${\operatorname{sp}} (c) = [0, 1]$.
Set $z = (x - \varepsilon)_{+} + c$.
Then $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (z)$ and
$(x - \varepsilon)_{+} \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} z \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} x$.

Define
\[
I = \bigl\{ j \in \{0, 1, \ldots, m\} \colon
 {\mbox{$\langle a_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}$
    is the class of a nonzero projection}} \bigr\}.
\]

For $j \not\in I$, set $b_j = a_j$.
Then $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (b_j)$.
Also,
\[
\langle z \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq \langle x \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
(k_j + 1) \langle x \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq k_j \langle b_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}.
\]
The second part says that $\langle z \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle b_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}$.

Now suppose $j \in I$.
We may as well assume that $a_j$ is a projection.
Use Lemma~\ref{L_5Z26_InterpPj} to choose
$b_j, s_j \in (a_j (M_{\infty} (A^{\alpha})) a_j)_{+}$ and $\delta \in (0, 1)$
such that
\[
{\operatorname{sp}} (b_j) = {\operatorname{sp}} (s_j) = [0, 1],
\qquad
b_j \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} a_j,
\]
\[
a_j \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} (b_j - \delta)_{+} \oplus (s_j - \delta)_{+},
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
2 k_j \langle s_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq \langle (b_j - \delta)_{+} \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}.
\]
Then
\[
\begin{split}
(2 k_j + 2) \langle z \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
& \leq (2 k_j + 2) \langle x \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
\\
& \leq 2 k_j \langle a_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
  \leq 2 k_j \langle b_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} + 2 k_j \langle s_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
  \leq (2 k_j + 1) \langle b_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}.
\end{split}
\]
Thus, here too $\langle z \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle b_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}$,
$0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (b_j)$,
and $\langle b_j \rangle_A \leq \langle a_j \rangle_A$ because $\langle b_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq \langle a_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}$.

Passing to~$A$, we have $\langle z \rangle_A <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle b_j \rangle_A$ for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$.
Since $A$ has $m$-comparison, it follows that
$\langle z \rangle_A \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle b_j \rangle_A$.
Since $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (z)$
and of ${\operatorname{sp}} (b_j)$ for $j = 0, 1, \ldots, m$,
Proposition~4.10 of~\cite{MohPhil2021TRP} implies that
$\langle z \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle b_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}$.
Therefore
\[
\langle (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
 \leq \langle z \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
 \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle b_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
 \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle a_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}.
\]

We have proved that for all $\varepsilon > 0$ we have
\[
\langle (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle a_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}.
\]
So $\langle x \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle a_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}$.
This completes the case that $\langle x \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}$ is not the class of a projection.

Now assume that $\langle x \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}$ is the class of a nonzero
projection in $M_{\infty} (A^{\alpha})$. By Theorem 3.1 of
\cite{MohPhil2021TRP}, $A^{\alpha}$ is simple. Now, use Lemma 2.6 of \cite{Phl40} to choose
$b \in (M_{\infty} (A^{\alpha}))_{+} \setminus \{0\}$ such that
$\langle b \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq \langle a_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}$ for
$j = 0, 1, \hdots, m$.
Set $k = \max ( k_0, k_1, \ldots, k_m )$.
By Lemma~\ref{L_3618_Lg36}, there exist $\mu, \eta \in {\operatorname{W}} (A^{\alpha})$,
both not the classes of projection{s},
such that
\[
\mu \leq \langle x \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq \mu + \eta
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
(2 k + 2) \eta \leq \langle b \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}.
\]
For $j = 0, 1, \hdots, m$, we then have
\[
\begin{split}
(2 k_j + 2) (\mu + \eta)
& \leq (2 k_j + 2) \langle x \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} + (2 k_j + 2) \eta
\\
& \leq 2 k_j \langle a_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} + \langle b \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
  \leq 2 k_j \langle a_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} + \langle a_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
  = (2 k_j + 1) \langle a_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}.
\end{split}
\]
Hence $\mu + \eta <_{\operatorname{s}} \langle a_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}$.
Since $\mu + \eta$ is not the class of a projection,
the previous case yields the second step in the calculation
\[
\langle x \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq \mu + \eta \leq \sum_{j = 0}^m \langle b_j \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}.
\]
This completes the proof that if $A$ has
$m$-comparison, then so does $A^{\alpha}$.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- lem-13: body_md -->
\label{L_6327_TRP_Mn}
Let $A$ be an infinite dimensional simple separable unital C*-algebra,
let $G$ be a second countable compact group, and let
$\alpha \colon G \to {\operatorname{Aut}} (A)$ be an action which has the
restricted tracial Rokhlin property with comparison.
Let $N \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$.
For every $\varepsilon > 0$, every $n \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$,
every compact subset $F_{1} \subseteq M_N (A)$,
every compact subset $F_2 \subseteq M_N (A^{\alpha})$,
every $x \in M_N (A)_{+} \setminus \{ 0 \}$,
and every $y \in M_N (A^{\alpha})_{+} \setminus \{ 0 \}$, there exist
a projection $p_0 \in A^{\alpha}$ and a
unital completely positive contractive map
$\psi_0 \colon A \to p_0 A^{\alpha} p_0$ such that,
taking $p = 1_{M_N} \otimes p_0 \in M_N (A^{\alpha})$
and $\psi = {\operatorname{id}}_{M_N} \otimes \psi_0 \colon M_N (A) \to p M_N (A^{\alpha}) p$,
the following hold:
 
\begin{enumerate}
 
\item\label{Item_1X07_18}
$\psi$ is an $(n, F_1 \cup F_2, \varepsilon)$-approximately
multiplicative map in the sense of Definition~1.5 of~\cite{MohPhil2021TRP}.
 
\item\label{commute1469}
$\| p a - a p \| < \varepsilon$
for all $a \in F_{1} \cup F_{2}$.
 
\item\label{Item_1X07_21}
$\| \psi (a) - p a p \| < \varepsilon$
for all $a \in F_2$.
 
\item\label{Item_1X07_19}
$1_{M_N (A)} - p \precsim_{A} x$.
 
\item\label{Item_1X07_20}
