$1_{M_N (A)} - p \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} y$.
 
\item\label{Item_1X07_20p}
$1_{M_N (A)} - p \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} p$.
 
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lem-13 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
We will systematically write elements $b \in M_n (A)$
as matrices $b = (b_{j, k})_{j, k = 1}^N$.
We also use standard matrix unit notation, so that
$(b_{j, k})_{j, k = 1}^N = \sum_{j, k = 1}^N e_{j, k} \otimes b_{j, k}$.
Define compact subsets $S_1 \subseteq A$ and $S_2 \subseteq A^{\alpha}$ by
\[
S_{\nu} = \bigl\{ a_{j, k} \colon
 {\mbox{$a \in F_{\nu}$ and $j, k \in \{ 1, 2, \ldots, N \}$}} \bigr\}
\]
for $\nu = 1, 2$.
Following the argument in the proof of
Proposition~4.19 of~\cite{MohPhil2021TRP}, use Lemma~3.8 of~\cite{PhlT1},
then Theorem 3.1 and Proposition 3.2 of \cite{MohPhil2021TRP}
and Lemma~2.4 of~\cite{Phl40},
to get $x_0 \in A_{+} \setminus \{ 0 \}$
and $y_0 \in (A^{\alpha})_{+} \setminus \{ 0 \}$ such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6410_Multiple}
1_{M_n} \otimes x_0 \precsim_{A} x
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
1_{M_n} \otimes y_0 \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} y.
\end{equation}
 
Further set
\[
\varepsilon_0 = \frac{\varepsilon}{N^{n + 1} + 1}.
\]
Apply Theorem~2.14 of~\cite{MohPhil2021TRP}
with $\varepsilon_0$ in place of~$\varepsilon$,
with $n$ as given,
with $S_1$ and $S_2$ in place of $F_1$ and~$F_2$,
and with $x_0$ and $y_0$ in place of $x$ and~$y$.
Let $p_0 \in A^{\alpha}$ and $\psi_0 \colon A \to p_0 A^{\alpha} p_0$
be the resulting projection{} and $(n, S_1 \cup S_2, \varepsilon_0)$-approximately
multiplicative unital completely positive contractive map.
Define $p = 1_{M_N} \otimes p_0$ and $\psi = {\operatorname{id}}_{M_N} \otimes \psi_0$.

We verify the conclusions of the lemma.
We start with~(\ref{Item_1X07_18}).
An argument by induction on $m$ shows that every
product $b = a_1 a_2 \cdots a_m$
with $a_1, a_2, \ldots, a_m \in F_1 \cup F_2$ has the form
$(b_{j, k})_{j, k = 1}^N$ in which, for $j, k = 1, 2, \ldots, N$,
the element $b_{j, k}$ is a sum of $N^{m - 1}$ products
of length~$m$ of elements of $S_1 \cup S_2$.
The matrix entries of $c = \psi (a_1) \psi (a_2) \cdots \psi (a_m)$
are sums of the same products, except that
each element $z \in S_1 \cup S_2$ is replaced with $\psi_0 (z)$.
When $m \in \{ 1, 2, \ldots, n \}$, the approximate multiplicativity
condition on $\psi_0$, used at the second step, implies that
\[
\begin{split}
\| (\psi (b) - c)_{j, k} \|
& = \| \psi_0 (b_{j, k}) - c_{j, k} \|
\\
& \leq N^{m - 1}
   \sup_{z_1, z_2, \ldots, z_m \in S_1 \cup S_2}
      \bigl\| \psi_0 (z_1 z_2 \cdots z_m)
            - \psi_0 (z_1) \psi_0 (z_2) \cdots \psi_0 (z_m) \bigr\|
\\
& \leq N^{m - 1} \varepsilon_0.
\end{split}
\]
Therefore
\[
\| \psi (b) - c \| \leq N^2 \cdot N^{m - 1} \varepsilon_0 < \varepsilon.
\]
This proves~(\ref{Item_1X07_18}).

For~(\ref{commute1469}),
let $a = (a_{j, k})_{j, k = 1}^N \in F_1 \cup F_2$.
Then $a_{j, k} \in S_1 \cup S_2$ for $j, k = 1, 2, \ldots, N$.
So $p a - a p = \bigl( p_0 a_{j, k} - a_{j, k} p_0 \bigr)_{j, k = 1}^N$,
whence
\[
\| p a - a p \|
 \leq \sum_{j, k = 1}^N \| p_0 a_{j, k} - a_{j, k} p_0 \|
 < N^2 \varepsilon_0 \leq \varepsilon.
\]
Part~(\ref{Item_1X07_21}) is similar:
if $a = (a_{j, k})_{j, k = 1}^N \in F_2$, then $a_{j, k} \in S_2$
for all $j, k$, so
\[
\| \psi (a) - p a p \|
  \leq \sum_{j, k = 1}^N \| \psi_0 (a_{j, k} ) - p_0 a_{j, k} p_0 \|
   < N^2 \varepsilon_0 \leq \varepsilon.
\]

For~(\ref{Item_1X07_19}), we have, using~(\ref{Eq_6410_Multiple})
at the last step,
\[
\langle 1_{M_N (A)} - p \rangle_A
 = N \langle 1 - p_0 \rangle_A
 \leq N \langle x_0 \rangle_A
 \leq \langle x \rangle_A,
\]
and (\ref{Item_1X07_20}) is proved similarly.
Condition~(\ref{Item_1X07_20p}) is immediate from
$1 - p_0 \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} p_0$.
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- thm-9: body_md -->
\label{Alm_Div_TRP}
Let $A$ be a stably finite simple separable infinite dimensional
unital C*-algebra,
let $G$ be a second countable compact group,
and let $\alpha \colon G \to {\operatorname{Aut}} (A)$ be an action
which has the restricted tracial Rokhlin property with comparison
but does not have the Rokhlin property.
Let $m \in \mathbb{N}$.
Suppose $A$ is $m$-almost divisible.
Then $A^{\alpha}$ is $(2 m + 1)$-almost divisible.
<!-- end thm-9 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
We verify the criterion of Proposition~\ref{P_6324_WDiv}.
Thus, let $n \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$, let $a \in M_{n} (A^{\alpha})_+$,
let $k \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}$, and let $\varepsilon > 0$.
Using the notation of Proposition~\ref{P_6324_WDiv}, we must find
$b \in M_{\infty} (A^{\alpha})_+$
such that in ${\operatorname{W}} (A^{\alpha})$ we have
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6325_Goal}
k \langle b \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq \langle a \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
\langle (a - \varepsilon)_+ \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq (k + 1) (2 m + 2) \langle b \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}.
\end{equation}
 
By Proposition~4.19 of~\cite{MohPhil2021TRP},
the action $g \mapsto {\operatorname{id}}_{M_n} \otimes \alpha_g$ of $G$ on $M_{n} \otimes A$
also has the restricted tracial Rokhlin property with comparison.
Therefore we may assume $n = 1$.
We may also clearly assume that $(a - \varepsilon)_+ \neq 0$.

First assume that $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (a)$.
In this case we will prove (\ref{Eq_6325_Goal}) with $m + 1$
in place of $2 m + 2$ in the second inequality.
Choose  $\rho \in (0, \frac{\varepsilon}{3})$ such that
${\operatorname{sp}} (x) \cap \bigl( \rho, \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \bigr) \neq \varnothing$.
Choose $\lambda \in {\operatorname{sp}} (x) \cap \bigl( \rho, \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \bigr)$.
Choose a continuous function{} $h \colon [0, \infty) \to [0, 1]$ such that
${\operatorname{supp}} (h) \subseteq \bigl( \rho, \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \bigr)$ and $h (\lambda) \neq 0$. By Theorem 3.1 and Proposition 3.2 of \cite{MohPhil2021TRP}, $A^{\alpha}$ is simple and not type I. Now, Use Lemma~2.4 of~\cite{Phl40} to find $t \in ({\overline{h (a) A^{\alpha} h (a)}})_{+} \setminus \{ 0 \}$ such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6325_t}
k \langle t \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq \langle h (a) \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}.
\end{equation}
 

Apply the other direction of Proposition~\ref{P_6324_WDiv} to~$A$.
We get $r \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$ and $c \in M_r (A)_{+}$ such that
in ${\operatorname{W}} (A)$ we have
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6324_c}
k \langle c \rangle_{A} \leq \left\langle  \left( a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ \right\rangle_{A}
\quad {\mbox{and}} \quad
\left\langle \left( a - \frac{2 \varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ \right\rangle_{A}
 \leq (k + 1) (m + 1) \langle c \rangle_{A}.
\end{equation}
 
Using Proposition~4.19 of~\cite{MohPhil2021TRP} again,
we may replace $A$ with $M_r (A)$,
and thus assume that both $a$ and~$c$ are in~$A$.

Define $N = (m + 1) (k + 1)$ and,
in the first of these using the inclusion in the upper left corner,
\[
x = 1_{k} \otimes c \in M_{k} (A) \subseteq M_N (A)
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
y = 1_{N} \otimes c \in M_N (A).
\]
Abusing notation, we also take $A \subseteq M_N (A)$
via the inclusion in the upper left corner.
Thus, for example, in the following, using standard matrix units in $M_N$,
$a$ is really $e_{1, 1} \otimes a$
and $c$ is really $e_{1, 1} \otimes c$.
Use~(\ref{Eq_6324_c}) to choose $w \in M_N (A)$ such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6325_wsyw}
\left\| w^* y w - \left( a - \frac{2 \varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ \right\|
  < \frac{\varepsilon}{12}.
\end{equation}
 
Define
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6325_dt}
\delta = \frac{\varepsilon}{24 \| w \|^2 + 1}.
\end{equation}
 
Use the first part of~(\ref{Eq_6324_c}) to choose $v \in M_N (A)$ such that
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6325_vsav}
\left\| v^* \left( a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ v - x \right\| < \delta.
\end{equation}
 
Define
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6325_rh}
\rho = \min \left( \frac{\varepsilon}{12}, \,  \frac{\delta}{\| v \|^2 + 1} \right).
\end{equation}
 

Define finite subsets $F_1 \subseteq M_N (A)$ and $F_2 \subseteq M_N (A^{\alpha})$ by
\[
F_1 = \bigl\{ c, v, w, x, y \bigr\}
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
F_2 = \left\{ \left( a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+, \,
          \left( a - \frac{2 \varepsilon}{3} \right)_+, \, (a - \varepsilon)_{+} \right\}.
\]
Apply Lemma~\ref{L_6327_TRP_Mn} with $\rho$ in place of~$\varepsilon$,
with $n = 3$, with $F_1$ and $F_2$ as given,
with $1_{M_N (A)}$ in place of~$x$,
and with $t$ (as in~(\ref{Eq_6325_t}), identified with $e_{1, 1} \otimes t$)
in place of~$y$,
getting a projection $p_0 \in A^{\alpha}$ and a
unital completely positive contractive map
$\psi_0 \colon A \to p_0 A^{\alpha} p_0$ such that,
taking $p = 1_{M_N} \otimes p_0 \in M_N (A^{\alpha})$
and $\psi = {\operatorname{id}}_{M_N} \otimes \psi \colon M_N (A) \to p M_N (A^{\alpha}) p$,
the following hold:
 
\begin{enumerate}
 
\item\label{I_6324_Item_1X07_18}
Whenever $m \in \{ 1, 2, 3 \}$
and $z_1, z_2, \ldots, z_m \in F_1 \cup F_2$, we have
\[
\bigl\| \psi (z_1 z_2 \cdots z_m)
  - \psi (z_1) \psi (z_2) \cdots \psi (z_m) \bigr\|
   < \rho.
\]
 
\item\label{I_6324_commute1469}
$\| p z - z p \| < \rho$ for all $z \in F_{1} \cup F_{2}$.
 
\item\label{I_6324_Item_1X07_21}
$\| \psi (z) - p z p \| < \rho$ for all $z \in F_2$.
 
\item\label{I_6324_Item_1X07_20}
$1 - p \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} t$.
\end{enumerate}
 
Given our identification of $A$ as the upper left corner of $M_N (A)$,
we are identifying $p_0 a p_0$ with $p a p$, $\psi (a)$ with $\psi_0 (a)$,
etc.

Define $b_0 = (\psi (c) - 2 \delta)_{+} \in A^{\alpha}$.
This gives, in $M_N (A^{\alpha})$,
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6325_b0_mult}
1_k \otimes b_0 = (\psi (x) - 2 \delta)_{+}
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
1_{(m + 1) (k + 1)} \otimes b_0 = (\psi (y) - 2 \delta)_{+}.
\end{equation}
 
Further define
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6325_b_Dfn}
b = b_0 \oplus t.
\end{equation}
 

We first claim that
\[
\left( p \left( a - \frac{2 \varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ p - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+
\precsim_{A^{\alpha}} (\psi (y) - 2 \delta)_{+}.
\]
To prove the claim, first estimate,
using (\ref{I_6324_Item_1X07_18}), (\ref{Eq_6325_wsyw}),
and (\ref{I_6324_Item_1X07_21}) at the second step,
and (\ref{Eq_6325_rh}) at the third step,
\[
\begin{split}
& \left\| \psi (w)^* \psi (y) \psi (w)
  - p \left( a - \frac{2 \varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ p \right\|
\\
& \hspace*{3em} {\mbox{}}
 \leq \| \psi (w)^* \psi (y) \psi (w) - \psi (w^* y w) \|
    + \left\| \psi \left( w^* y w
         - \left( a - \frac{2 \varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ \right) \right\|
 \\
& \hspace*{6em} {\mbox{}}
  + \left\| \psi \left( \left( a - \frac{2 \varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ \right)
     - p \left( a - \frac{2 \varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ p \right\|
\\
& < \rho + \frac{\varepsilon}{12} + \rho
  \leq \frac{\varepsilon}{4}.
\end{split}
\]
Since $\| (\psi (y) - 2 \delta)_{+} - \psi (y) \| \leq 2 \delta$,
we then get, using (\ref{Eq_6325_dt}) at the third step,
\[
\begin{split}
& \left\| \psi (w)^* (\psi (y) - 2 \delta)_{+} \psi (w)
  - p \left( a - \frac{2 \varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ p \right\|
\\
& \hspace*{3em} {\mbox{}}
 \leq 2 \delta \| w \|^2
 + \left\| \psi (w)^* \psi (y) \psi (w)
  - p \left( a - \frac{2 \varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ p \right\|
 < \frac{\varepsilon}{12} + \frac{\varepsilon}{4}
  = \frac{\varepsilon}{3}.
\end{split}
\]
Therefore
\[
\left( p \left( a - \frac{2 \varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ p - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+
\precsim_{A^{\alpha}} \psi (w)^* (\psi (y) - 2 \delta)_{+} \psi (w)
\precsim_{A^{\alpha}} (\psi (y) - 2 \delta)_{+},
\]
proving the claim.

Our second claim is that
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6325_bsub}
\langle (a - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq N \langle b \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}.
\end{equation}
 
To prove this, use Lemma~1.8 of~\cite{Phl40} at the second step,
the previous claim and (\ref{I_6324_Item_1X07_20}) at the third step,
and (\ref{Eq_6325_b0_mult}) at the fourth step,
getting
\[
\begin{split}
(a - \varepsilon)_{+}
& = \left( \left( a - \frac{2 \varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+
  \precsim_{A^{\alpha}}
 \left( p \left( a - \frac{2 \varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ p - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+
 \oplus (1 - p)
\\
& \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} (\psi (y) - 2 \delta)_{+} \oplus t
  = 1_{N} \otimes b_0 \oplus t
  \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} 1_{N} \otimes (b_0 \oplus t)
  = 1_{N} \otimes b.
\end{split}
\]
The claim follows.

This claim is the second part of~(\ref{Eq_6325_Goal}),
with $m + 1$ in place of $2 m + 2$.

We next claim that
 
\begin{equation}\label{Eq_6325_xsubb}
(\psi (x) - 2 \delta)_{+} \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} \left( a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+.
\end{equation}
 
Using $\psi (v) \in p M_N (A^{\alpha}) p$ at the first step,
using (\ref{I_6324_Item_1X07_21}), (\ref{I_6324_Item_1X07_18}),
and (\ref{Eq_6325_vsav}) at the third step,
and using (\ref{Eq_6325_rh}) at the fourth step,
we get
\[
\begin{split}
& \left\| \psi (v)^* \left( a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ \psi (v)
    - \psi (x) \right\|
\\
& \hspace*{3em} {\mbox{}}
 = \left\| \psi (v)^* p \left( a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ p \psi (v)
    - \psi (x) \right\|
\\
& \hspace*{3em} {\mbox{}} \leq \| \psi (v) \|^2
  \left\| p \left( a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ p
    - \psi \left( \left( a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ \right) \right\|
\\
& \hspace*{6em} {\mbox{}}
  + \left\|
    \psi (v)^* \psi \left( \left( a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ \right) \psi (v)
    - \psi \left( v^* \left( a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ v \right) \right\|
\\
& \hspace*{6em} {\mbox{}}
  + \left\| v^* \left( a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ v - x \right\|
\\
& \leq \| v \|^2 \rho + \rho + \delta \leq 2 \delta.
\end{split}
\]
Therefore
\[
( \psi (x) - 2 \delta)_{+}
 \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} \psi (v)^* \left( a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+ \psi (v)
 \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} \left( a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+,
\]
as claimed.

We now use~(\ref{Eq_6325_b_Dfn})~at the first step,
(\ref{Eq_6325_b0_mult}) at the second step,
the previous claim and~(\ref{Eq_6325_t}) at the third step,
and $h (a) \perp \left( a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+$ at the fourth step,
to get
\[
\begin{split}
1_{k} \otimes b
& = 1_{k} \otimes (b_0 \oplus t)
\\
& = (\psi (x) - 2 \delta)_{+} \oplus 1_{k} \otimes t
  \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} \left( a - \frac{\varepsilon}{3} \right)_+
    \oplus h (a)
  \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} a.
\end{split}
\]
This is the first inequality in~(\ref{Eq_6325_Goal}),
and completes the proof when $0$ is a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (a)$.

Now assume $0$ is not a limit point of ${\operatorname{sp}} (a)$.
We may assume that $a$ is a projection.
Lemma~\ref{L_5Z26_InterpPj} yields
$x, y \in (a (A^{\alpha} \otimes K) a)_{+} \subseteq M_{\infty} (A^{\alpha})$
and $\varepsilon \in (0, 1)$
such that ${\operatorname{sp}} (x) = {\operatorname{sp}} (y) = [0, 1]$,
$a \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \oplus (y - \varepsilon)_{+}$,
and $y \precsim_{A^{\alpha}} (x - \varepsilon)_{+}$.
Applying the case already done to~$x$,
we get $b \in M_{\infty} (A^{\alpha})_+$ such that
\[
k \langle b \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq \langle x \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
\qquad {\mbox{and}} \qquad
\langle (x - \varepsilon)_+ \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq (k + 1) (m + 1) \langle b \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}.
\]
Now
\[
k \langle b \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq \langle x \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} \leq \langle a \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
\]
and, at the first step using the fact that $a$ is a projection,
\[
\begin{split}
\langle (a - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
& = \langle a \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
  \leq \langle (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_{A^{\alpha}} + \langle y \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
\\
& \leq 2 \langle (x - \varepsilon)_{+} \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}
  \leq 2 (k + 1) (m + 1) \langle b \rangle_{A^{\alpha}}.
\end{split}
\]
This completes the proof that $A^{\alpha}$ is $(2 m + 1)$-almost divisible.
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- thm-10: body_md -->
\label{T_6326_TRP_pure}
Let $A$ be a simple unital separable infinite dimensional stably finite
C*-algebra.
Let $\alpha \colon G \to {\operatorname{Aut}}(A)$ be an action of a second countable
compact group which has the tracial Rokhlin property with comparison
but does not have the Rokhlin property.
If $A$ is pure, then the crossed product
$C^* \bigl( G, \, A, \, \alpha \bigr)$ is pure.
<!-- end thm-10 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
It follows from Proposition~\ref{Alm_Div_TRP},
Proposition~\ref{ComparisonTRP},
and Theorem~\ref{T_PureEquiv} that $A^{\alpha}$ is pure.
Now, Corollary~3.9 of~\cite{MohPhil2021TRP} implies that
$C^* \bigl( G, \, A, \, \alpha \bigr)$ is strongly
Morita equivalent to $A^{\alpha}$. Therefore
$C^* \bigl( G, \, A, \, \alpha \bigr)$ is pure.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- ntn-4: body_md -->
\label{N_1_QFreeFn}
For $n \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0} \cup \{ \infty \}$, we let $F_n$ denote the free
group on $n$~generators.
We let $u_1, u_2, \ldots, u_n \in C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_n)$
(when $n = \infty$,
$u_1, u_2, \ldots \in C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{\infty})$)
be the ``standard'' unitaries in $C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_n)$,
obtained as the images of the standard generators of~$F_n$.
For $\zeta = (\zeta_1, \zeta_2, \ldots, \zeta_n) \in (S^1)^n$
(when $n = \infty$,
for $\zeta = (\zeta_n)_{n \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}} \in (S^1)^{{\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}}$), we let
$\varphi_{\zeta} \in {\operatorname{Aut}} ( C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_n) )$ be the
(quasifree) automorphism determined by
$\varphi_{\zeta} (u_k) = \zeta_k u_k$ for $k = 1, 2, \ldots, n$
(when $n = \infty$, for $k = 1, 2, \ldots)$.
It is well known
that $\zeta \mapsto \varphi_{\zeta}$ is continuous.
<!-- end ntn-4 -->
<!-- ntn-5: body_md -->
\label{N_2_FreeShift}
Take the standard generators of the free group $F_{\infty}$
to be indexed by~${\mathbb{Z}}$, and for $n \in {\mathbb{Z}}$ let
$u_n \in C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{\infty})$ be the unitary
obtained as the image of the corresponding generator
of~$F_{\infty}$.
We denote by $\sigma$ the free shift on
$C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{\infty})$, that is, the automorphism
$\sigma \in {\operatorname{Aut}} ( C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{\infty}) )$ determined
by $\sigma (u_n) = u_{n + 1}$ for $n \in {\mathbb{Z}}$.
<!-- end ntn-5 -->
<!-- prp-9: body_md -->
\label{P_FreePure}
For $n \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0} \cup \{ \infty \}$, let $F_n$ be the free
group on $n$~generators. Then $C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_n)$ is pure.
<!-- end prp-9 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
Theorem~A of~\cite{AGKEP} implies that
$C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_n)$ has strict comparison.
Pureness follows by Theorem~5.13 of~\cite{AnPrTlVt}, as
described in Example~5.14 there.
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- exa-1: body_md -->
\label{E_1_ShiftAndFInf}
Set $X_0 = (S^1)^{{\mathbb{Z}}}$.
Let $h_0 \colon X_0 \to X_0$ be the
forward shift, defined for $\zeta = (\zeta_n)_{n \in {\mathbb{Z}}} \in X_0$
by $h_0 (\zeta) = (\zeta_{n - 1})_{n \in {\mathbb{Z}}}$.
Let $D = C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{\infty})$.
Choose a bijection $\sigma \colon {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0} \to {\mathbb{Z}}$, and for
$\zeta = (\zeta_n)_{n \in {\mathbb{Z}}} \in X_0$ let
$\alpha_{\zeta}^{(0)} \in {\operatorname{Aut}} (D)$ be the automorphism $\varphi_{\zeta}$ of
Notation~\ref{N_1_QFreeFn}.
Then $\zeta \mapsto \alpha_{\zeta}^{(0)}$ is continuous.

We have ${\operatorname{mdim}} (h_0) = 1$ by Proposition~3.3 of~\cite{LndWs},
and we can use $X_0$, $h_0$, $D$, and
$\zeta \mapsto \alpha^{(0)}_{\zeta}$ in Lemma~\ref{L_1_GetAuto}.
However, $h_0$ is not minimal.
We therefore proceed as in Example~6.3 of \cite{ArBcPh2}.
Identify $[0, 1]$ with a closed arc in~$S^1$,
say via $\lambda \mapsto \exp (\pi i \lambda)$.
Use this to identify
$[0, 1]^{{\mathbb{Z}}}$ with a closed subset of $X_0 = (S^1)^{{\mathbb{Z}}}$.
This identification is equivariant when both spaces are
equipped with the shift homeomorphism{s}.
Let $X \subseteq X_0$ and
$h = h_0 |_{X} \colon X \to X$ be the minimal subshift
in~\cite{GlKr}, which can be taken to have mean dimension
arbitrarily close to~$1$ (by Proposition 3.3
of~\cite{LndWs} and the argument in the proof
of Proposition 3.5 of~\cite{LndWs}).
For $\zeta \in X$, let
$\alpha_{\zeta} = \alpha_{\zeta}^{(0)}$.
Let
$\alpha \colon {\mathbb{Z}} \to {\operatorname{Aut}} (C (X, D))$ be the corresponding
action as in Lemma~\ref{L_1_GetAuto}.
Then $\alpha$ lies
over the free minimal action of ${\mathbb{Z}}$ on~$X$ generated by~$h$.

The crossed product
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$ is simple
by Proposition~1.6 of \cite{ArBcPh2} and $D$ is pure by
Proposition~\ref{P_FreePure},
so the crossed product is pure by Theorem~\ref{T_4_PureCrossProd}
and has stable rank one by
Corollary~\ref{C_5_CrossProdSR1}.
<!-- end exa-1 -->
<!-- exa-2: body_md -->
\label{E_2_ShiftAndFInf}
Let $X_0$, $h_0$, $X$, and $h$
be as in Example~\ref{E_1_ShiftAndFInf}.
Let $D_0 = \bigotimes_{n \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}} M_2$ be the $2^{\infty}$~UHF algebra.
Choose a bijection $\sigma \colon {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0} \to {\mathbb{Z}}$,
and for $\zeta = (\zeta_n)_{n \in {\mathbb{Z}}} \in X_0$ define
\[
\alpha_{\zeta}^{(0)}
 = \bigotimes_{n \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}}
  {\operatorname{Ad}} \left( \left( \begin{matrix}
       1 & 0 \\
       0 & \zeta_{\sigma (n)}
     \end{matrix} \right) \right)
 \in {\operatorname{Aut}} (D_0).
\]
Then $\zeta \mapsto \alpha_{\zeta}^{(0)}$ is continuous.
Set $D = D_0 \ast_{r} C ([0, 1])$,
taking the reduced free product to be amalgamated over ${\mathbb{C}} \cdot 1$
and with respect to the unique tracial state{} on~$D_0$
and the Lebesgue measure state on $C ([0, 1])$.
For $\zeta \in X$,
define $\alpha_{\zeta} = \alpha_{\zeta}^{(0)} \ast_{r} {\operatorname{id}}_{C ([0, 1])}$.
The crossed product $C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$ is
simple by Proposition~1.6 of \cite{ArBcPh2}.
Examples 3 and 8 of~\cite{HKER}, together with Theorem~B there,
imply that $D$ is selfless in the sense of Definition~2.1
of~\cite{Rob}. Theorem~3.1 of~\cite{Rob} implies that $D$ has
stable rank one, strict comparison and a unique normalized
2-quasitrace. It follows that $D$ is pure by Theorem~5.13
of~\cite{AnPrTlVt}, and so the crossed product is pure by
Theorem~\ref{T_4_PureCrossProd} and has stable rank one
by Corollary~\ref{C_5_CrossProdSR1}.
<!-- end exa-2 -->
<!-- exa-3: body_md -->
\label{E_3_ShiftAndFInf}
Let $X_0$, $h_0$, $D$, and $\zeta \mapsto \alpha_{\zeta}^{(0)}$
be as in Example~\ref{E_1_ShiftAndFInf}.
We will, however, use the homeomorphism{} $h_0^2$ of~$X_0$,
which is the shift on $(S^1 \times S^1)^{{\mathbb{Z}}}$.
Let $(X, h)$ be the minimal subspace of the shift on
$([0, 1]^2)^{{\mathbb{Z}}}$
constructed in Proposition 3.5 of \cite{LndWs},
which satisfies ${\operatorname{mdim}} (h) > 1$.
Use an embedding of $[0, 1]^2$ in~$(S^1)^2$
to choose an equivariant homeomorphism{} $g$
from $(X, h)$ to an invariant closed subset of $(X_0, h_0^2)$.
For $x \in X$, let $\alpha_{x} = \alpha_{g (x)}^{(0)}$.
Let $\alpha \colon {\mathbb{Z}} \to {\operatorname{Aut}} (C (X, D))$
be the corresponding action as in Lemma~\ref{L_1_GetAuto}.
The crossed product
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D), \, \alpha \bigr)$ is
simple by Proposition~1.6 of \cite{ArBcPh2} and $D$ is
pure, so the crossed product is pure by Theorem~\ref{T_4_PureCrossProd}
and has stable rank one by
Corollary~\ref{C_5_CrossProdSR1}.
<!-- end exa-3 -->
<!-- exa-4: body_md -->
\label{E_4_FreeShift}
Let $X$ be any compact metric space, and let $h$ be an
arbitrary minimal homeomorphism of~$X$.
Let
$\sigma \in {\operatorname{Aut}} ( C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{\infty}) )$ be as
in Notation~\ref{N_2_FreeShift}.
Let
$\alpha \in {\operatorname{Aut}} \bigl( C (X) \otimes C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{\infty}) \bigr)$
be the tensor product of the automorphism
$f \mapsto f \circ h^{-1}$ of $C (X)$ and~$\sigma$.
Then
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X) \otimes C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{\infty}),
  \, \alpha \bigr)$
is simple by Proposition~1.6 of \cite{ArBcPh2}
and $D$ is pure by Proposition~\ref{P_FreePure},
so the crossed product is pure by Theorem~\ref{T_4_PureCrossProd}
and has stable rank one by Corollary~\ref{C_5_CrossProdSR1}.

If $C^* ({\mathbb{Z}}, X, h)$ has real rank zero,
for example if $h$ is an irrational rotation, then
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X) \otimes C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{\infty}),
  \, \alpha \bigr)$
has real rank zero by Corollary~\ref{C_6327_RRZ}.
<!-- end exa-4 -->
<!-- exa-5: body_md -->
\label{E_5_IrrRot}
Fix any $n \in \left\{  2, 3, \ldots, \infty  \right\}$ and take
$D = C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{n})$.
Adopt Notation~\ref{N_1_QFreeFn}.
Take $X = S^{1}$, let
$\theta \in {\mathbb{R}} \setminus {\mathbb{Q}}$ and let $h \colon X \to X$ be the
irrational rotation  $\zeta \mapsto e^{2 \pi i \theta} \zeta$.
Following Notation~\ref{N_1_QFreeFn} for the
generators of~$D$, for $\zeta \in X$ let
$\alpha_{\zeta} \in {\operatorname{Aut}} (D)$ be determined by
$\alpha_{\zeta} (u_k) = \zeta u_k$ for
$k = 1, 2, \ldots, n$ (or $k \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$ if $n = \infty$).
Apply Lemma~\ref{L_1_GetAuto} to get an action
$\alpha \colon {\mathbb{Z}} \to {\operatorname{Aut}} \big( C (X, D ) \big)$.
The algebra
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D ), \, \alpha \bigr)$
is simple by Proposition~1.6 of \cite{ArBcPh2} and
$D$ is pure by Proposition~\ref{P_FreePure},
so the crossed product is pure by
Theorem~\ref{T_4_PureCrossProd} and has stable
rank one by Corollary~\ref{C_5_CrossProdSR1}.
It is well known that the irrational rotation
algebra $C^* ( {\mathbb{Z}}, X, h )$ has real rank zero.
(The range of the canonical map is the set
$\left\{ m + n \theta \colon m, n, \in {\mathbb{Z}} \right\}$, which is
dense in ${\mathbb{R}}$ since $\theta$ is irrational.)
It follows from Corollary~\ref{C_6327_RRZ} that
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D ), \, \alpha \bigr)$ has real rank zero.
<!-- end exa-5 -->
<!-- exa-6: body_md -->
\label{E_6_MultiFrst}
Fix $n \in \{ 2, 3, \ldots, \infty \}$ and take
$D = C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{n})$.
Fix $\theta_1, \theta_2, \ldots, \theta_n \in {\mathbb{R}}$
(or $\theta_1, \theta_2, \ldots \in {\mathbb{R}}$)
such that $1, \theta_1, \theta_2, \ldots, \theta_n$
(or $1, \theta_1, \theta_2, \ldots$)
are linearly independent over~${\mathbb{Q}}$.
Take $X = (S^{1})^{n}$, and let $h \colon X \to X$ be
\[
h (\lambda_1, \lambda_2, \ldots, \lambda_n)
 = \bigl( e^{2 \pi i \theta_1} \lambda_1, \, e^{2 \pi i \theta_2} \lambda_2,
 \, \ldots, \, e^{2 \pi i \theta_n} \lambda_n \bigr)
\]
for $\lambda_1, \lambda_2, \ldots, \lambda_n \in S^1$,
or, if $n = \infty$,
\[
h (\lambda_1, \lambda_2, \ldots)
 = \bigl( e^{2 \pi i \theta_1} \lambda_1, \, e^{2 \pi i \theta_2} \lambda_2,
 \, \ldots, \bigr)
\]
for $\lambda_1, \lambda_2, \ldots \in S^1$.
Following
Notation~\ref{N_1_QFreeFn} for the generators of~$D$,
for $\zeta \in X$ let $\alpha_{\zeta} \in {\operatorname{Aut}} (D)$
(called $\varphi_{\zeta}$ in Notation~\ref{N_1_QFreeFn}) be
determined by $\alpha_{\zeta} (u_k) = \zeta_k u_k$ for
$k = 1, 2, \ldots, n$ (or $k \in {\mathbb{Z}}_{> 0}$ if $n = \infty$).
Apply Lemma~\ref{L_1_GetAuto} to get an action
$\alpha \colon
{\mathbb{Z}} \to {\operatorname{Aut}} \bigl( C (X, \, C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{n}) ) \bigr)$.
Then
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, \, C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{n}) ), \, \alpha \bigr)$
is simple, pure, and has stable rank one as in
Example~\ref{E_5_IrrRot}.
The transformation group C*-algebra
$C^* ( {\mathbb{Z}}, X, h )$ again has real rank zero.
The range of the canonical map
$\rho \colon K_{0} (C^* ( {\mathbb{Z}}, X, h )) \to {\operatorname{Aff}} (T (C^* ( {\mathbb{Z}}, X, h )))$
is the set
\[
\left\{ m_0 + m_1 \theta_1 + \cdots + m_n \theta_n
\colon m_0, m_1, \ldots, m_n \in {\mathbb{Z}} \right\},
\]
(analogously if $n = \infty$)
which is dense in ${\mathbb{R}}$ so long as $\theta_k$ is irrational
for at least one~$k$.
(Linear independence over ${\mathbb{Q}}$ of
$1, \theta_1, \theta_2, \ldots, \theta_n$, or of $1, \theta_1, \theta_2, \ldots$,
is needed for minimality, but not here.)
It follows from Corollary~\ref{C_6327_RRZ} that
$C^* \bigl( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D ), \, \alpha \bigr)$ has real rank zero.
<!-- end exa-6 -->
<!-- exa-7: body_md -->
\label{E_7_CSrFGp_Furst}
Let $X$ be any compact metric space, and let $h$ be an
arbitrary minimal homeomorphism of~$X$.
Following Notation~\ref{N_1_QFreeFn} for the generators
of $C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{2})$, let
$\gamma \in {\operatorname{Aut}} ( C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{2}) )$ be
given by $\gamma (u_1) = u_1$ and $\gamma (u_2) = u_1 u_2$.
Let
$\alpha \in {\operatorname{Aut}} \big( C (X) \otimes C^*_{\mathrm{r}} (F_{2}) \big)$
be the tensor product of the automorphism
$f \mapsto f \circ h^{-1}$ of $C (X)$ and~$\gamma$.
The algebra
$C^* \big( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D ), \, \alpha \big)$
is simple by Proposition~1.6 of \cite{ArBcPh2} and
$D$ is pure by Proposition~\ref{P_FreePure},
so $C^* \big( {\mathbb{Z}}, \, C (X, D ), \, \alpha \big)$ is pure by
Theorem~\ref{T_4_PureCrossProd} and has stable rank
one by Corollary~\ref{C_5_CrossProdSR1}.
<!-- end exa-7 -->
